With the 54 queue-4 candidates and the 17 skipped queue-1 candidates turned into classes by the measured rates (natural / induced / total, projected): Class 1 53.4 / 11.0 / 64.4, Class 2 0 / 4.9 / 4.9, Class 3 20.4 / 4.0 / 24.4, Class 4 16.5 / 2.3 / 18.8, Class 5 1.0 / 19.0 / 20.0, Class 6 1.0 / 13.3 / 14.3, Class 7 47.9 / 9.2 / 57.1. **Class 2 (4, projected about 5) and Class 6 (9, projected about 14) remain the thin classes; Class 5 is 19 of 20 induced.** The Class 7 count (47) is provisional in both directions: its 39 queue-1 errors are all in queue 5 and may change, and the 50 queue-2 Class 7 errors are held out until queue 5 decides them (D-024). **Correct examples in test: 159** (`test_real` 77 + human-confirmed queue 2: 82, natural 60 and induced 22). A queue-5 "correct" verdict on one of the 37 read-only calls would add to that number.

**Update (2026-11-15) — the correct-example pool from failed runs is less reliable than assumed.** The "correct" calls taken from failed runs were assumed correct because they come before the first call the labeler flagged as incorrect (natural: gpt-5-mini's localization; induced: the auto-label). Queue 2 checked 150 of them by hand: 148 decided (2 skipped), **82 confirmed correct and 66 judged wrong**. By origin: natural 40 of 100 wrong (60 confirmed), induced 26 of 48 wrong (22 confirmed). In other words the labeler's "first incorrect call" was often too late: in 45% of the checked cases an earlier call was already wrong. Of the 66, 50 were marked Class 7, 37 are read-only calls and 53 are in queue 5 for re-review under the labeling guide (D-024), so **66/148 is an upper bound on the unreliability**: if the re-review turns many read-only "wrong" calls back to correct, it falls; the 13 queue-2 errors outside queue 5 (classes 1 to 6) are the floor, 9% of 148.

Consequences. (1) **Only human-confirmed correct calls are used as correct examples in the test set** (queue 2 `correct`, plus `test_real`); the 566 unreviewed test-split calls stay out, and so do all 1,170 unreviewed train-split ones by default (`--train-positives accepted`). (2) `--train-positives gated` cannot be justified: its rule needs at least 90% confirmed, and the measured share is 60% natural and 46% induced. (3) Any earlier statement that failed-run calls before the first flagged call are "correct" holds for about 55% of them, not for all; this applies to the correct examples in D-018 and D-019 and to the per-class false-flag rates that rely on them. (4) The wrong ones are not wasted: they are real agent errors that a human found, now in the test set as `found_in: "queue2"` (D-024).

**Update (2026-11-15) — queue 5 is applied by rule, not by hand.** Decision: skip the human review of queue 5 (100 items) and apply it automatically. `python data/apply_queue5_rules.py apply` writes `data/derived/review_queue_rule_decisions.jsonl`, one record per queue-5 item in the shape of a human queue-5 decision, each marked `source: "rule"` and `rule: A | B | C | D`. **The original decisions file is never edited**; `data/review_decisions.py` reads the rule file beside it, a human queue-5 decision (a key pressed in `review --queue 5`) still wins over the rule record of the same item, and `python data/apply_queue5_rules.py undo` deletes the file and reverses every rule.

Rules and what they did to the 100 items:
- **A** (34 items): queue-2 "wrong, Class 7" on a read-only call becomes **correct**; tagged `parallel_lookup` (26) when calls were directly before it with no tool result between (the structural check on the stored window), plain correct (8) otherwise.
- **B** (1): the queue-1 `can_send_mms` Class 7 item is handled the same way. It was an error candidate, so it leaves the test errors; it is not added as a correct example (the builder adds correct examples only from queue 2).
- **C** (3): read-only items marked wrong with another class (3, 4, 4) keep your original decision; they no longer wait for queue 5, so they count as test errors.
- **D** (62): Class 7 decisions on write calls (54) and the 8 `test_real` Class 7 rows stand; `class7_reason` comes from the heuristic pre-fill with `class7_reason_source: "heuristic"`: `ineligible_state` 19 and `no_confirmation` 7 and `missing_step` 4 and `parallel_write` 3 and **`unlabeled` 29** (the heuristic found no signal). Two items already had a human queue-5 decision (airline task 36: a lookup judged correct, a basic-economy flight change judged `ineligible_state`); both agree with the rule and stay human.

Test set after the rules (`build_final_dataset.py` dry run and `coverage_projection.py` agree: 166 incorrect + 193 correct = 359 test rows, nothing held for queue 5, no duplicate test calls):

| class | test_real | queue 1 | queue 2 | queue 4 | total | natural | induced |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 wrong arg | 6 | 28 | 5 | 0 | 39 | 31 | 8 |
| 2 stale state | 0 | 3 | 1 | 0 | 4 | 0 | 4 |
| 3 wrong tool | 3 | 5 | 5 | 0 | 13 | 9 | 4 |
| 4 repeated call | 7 | 9 | 3 | 0 | 19 | 16 | 3 |
| 5 failure to propagate | 1 | 19 | 0 | 0 | 20 | 1 | 19 |
| 6 ignored error | 0 | 7 | 2 | 0 | 9 | 1 | 8 |
| 7 policy violation | 8 | 38 | 16 | 0 | 62 | 44 | 18 |
| all | 25 | 109 | 32 | 0 | 166 | 102 | 64 |

**Correct examples in test: 193** (`test_real` 77 + queue 2: 116, natural 94 and induced 22; the 34 are the read-only calls of rule A, 26 of them tagged `parallel_lookup`). The earlier table in this entry (148 errors, 159 correct) is superseded.

**Reporting.** The final-mode report now gives **overall Class 7 recall as the number to quote** and prints recall by sub-reason as "heuristic tags, approximate" (`notebooks/add_class7_report.py`, regenerated into the four notebooks; checked on made-up rows only). Reading the sub-reason cells: 29 of the 62 test Class 7 rows are `unlabeled`, and most reasons are only a structural guess, so no sub-reason cell is a measured number.

**Caveats.** (1) Rules A to D replace a human judgement with a rule the labeling guide states; the correct examples they add (34) are calls you had marked wrong, so their correctness rests on the guide's read-only rule and not on a fresh look. If the guide is wrong about some lookup (one that a policy forbids), the test set has a mislabeled correct example. (2) Class 7 is now the largest test class (62; 44 natural, 18 induced), 22 of its rows come from queue 2. (3) The `parallel_lookup` tag uses calls directly before the judged one; a lookup issued beside a write counts as parallel too. (4) The 8 `test_real` Class 7 rows keep the phase-4 hand label, which predates the guide.

**Update (2026-11-16) — the human review is complete; natural Class 7 stays auto-accepted although it is under the 70% gate.** Queues 1, 2 and 4 are decided by hand (queue 4: 29 accepted, 2 reclassified, 11 rejected, 12 skipped), queue 5 was applied by rule, queue 3 (the train audit) was not done. With the queue-4 decisions and the queue-5 rule in, the gate table is:

| (origin, class) | confirmed same class | gate |
|---|---|---|
| induced 2 | 3/16 (19%) | dropped unless reviewed |
| induced 5 | 19/19 (100%) | auto-accepted |
| induced 6 | 12/21 (57%) | dropped unless reviewed |
| natural 1 | 11/11 (100%) | auto-accepted |
| natural 3 | 25/45 (56%) | dropped unless reviewed |
| natural 4 | 8/11 (73%) | auto-accepted |
| natural 5 | 0/9 (0%) | dropped unless reviewed |
| natural 6 | 1/1 (1 reviewed) | dropped unless reviewed |
| **natural 7** | **28/41 (68%)** | **auto-accepted, by decision (below)** |

**Decision.** Natural Class 7 stays in `TRAIN_AUTO_ACCEPT` although 68% is just under the 70% rule (it was 29/41 before the queue-5 rule turned one read-only item, `can_send_mms`, from Class 7 into "not an error"). **Reason:** without it the Class 7 training rows are almost only the synthetic retail state flips (160 train rows), while the real Class 7 test errors are mostly telecom and airline; a model trained that way would see no real Class 7 at all. **Estimated label noise in those training rows: about a third.** Of the 41 natural Class 7 candidates reviewed (test split, used as the estimate for the train split), 28 were confirmed Class 7, 7 were really Class 1, 2 were Class 3 and 4 were not errors: so about 32% carry the wrong class, and about 10% are not errors at all (the binary label "incorrect" is right for about 90%). The train-split candidates are 26 before the held-out drop; at these rates about 8 of them have the wrong class and 2 or 3 are not errors. The estimate comes from 41 items, so it is rough (a 95% interval for 28/41 would run roughly 52% to 81%). **All other gates stay as they are.**

**A note on the positive gate.** The same table now shows the natural queue-2 share confirmed correct as 94/100, above the 90% rule of `--train-positives gated`. That figure includes the 34 calls the queue-5 rule turned from "wrong" to "correct" (60 of 100 before the rule), so it rests on a rule, not on a human look. The default `--train-positives accepted` is unchanged and the 993 natural and 177 induced train-split correct examples stay out.

**Update (2026-11-16, later) — the final build failed its duplicate check; the line-probe rule, content de-duplication and queue 6.** The first write of the final dataset (`data/check_final_dataset.py`: counts, leakage, duplicates, baselines) passed leakage and failed the duplicate check with 3 groups of identical content: (1) a natural train row (Class 7, `can_send_mms`, empty history) and a test row (correct, human, queue 2) with the same content, (2) two test rows with the same content and the same label (Class 1), (3) two test rows with the same content and conflicting labels, Class 1 from queue 1 and Class 3 from queue 4. Group 3 exposed a larger inconsistency: 43 candidates in queues 1 and 4 were first lookups of `L1001` (`get_details_by_id`) while the reported number was one of three lines, decided 17 accepted as Class 3, 9 as Class 1, 1 as Class 4, 8 rejected, 8 skipped; you had rejected the same pattern as correct in later items.

Decisions. **(a)** A train or validation row with the content of a test or test_synth row is dropped; the human test label wins (group 1: one Class 7 train row dropped). **(b)** Identical rows inside test and inside test_synth are kept once (test_real first, then queue 1 / 4, then queue 2); a group with conflicting labels is listed and never resolved automatically (`dedupe_content` in the builder; group 2: one Class 1 test row removed). **(c) The line-probe rule** (labeling guide, "Line lookups"): if the target line was identifiable before the call, a lookup of a different line is Class 1 and of the same line is correct; if it was not knowable, a lookup to find out which line is affected is correct; never Class 3. It is implemented structurally (`data/line_probe_rule.py`: phone numbers and line ids in the user's messages, line records with `line_id` and `phone_number` in earlier results, a single-line customer) and applied as rule decisions in `data/derived/review_queue_lineprobe_decisions.jsonl` (ids `lp::<original id>`, `source: "rule"`; `python data/apply_line_probe_rule.py undo` reverses it; the original decisions are untouched). **(d)** What it could not decide goes to queue 6 for a human (`review_queues.py add-queue6`, screen shows the rule, the evidence and the earlier decision).

Results on the 58 line-id lookups found (53 in queues 1, 2 and 4, 5 in `test_real`): **51 got a rule decision, all "correct"**: 46 first lookups of `L1001`, 2 of `L1002` after `L1001` showed another number, 1 of `L1003`, plus 2 `test_real` correct rows that stay correct. In every case the customer record listed line ids with no phone numbers, so the line was not knowable. **No case fell under "knowable", so the Class 1 branch never fired, and queue 6 is empty (0 items).** 7 lookups repeat an earlier identical lookup and keep their original decision. Effect on the test set: 26 errors you had accepted (17 Class 3, 9 Class 1) are no longer errors; skipped and rejected ones stay out. (The test set before this was 390 rows, 197 incorrect; after the rule, the content de-duplication and the 8 `test_real` Class 7 rows, it is 363 rows, 170 incorrect, 193 correct.) **Caveat:** the rule decides by structure only; a "knowable" case needs a line record or a stated line id before the call, and none of the 58 had one, so the Class 1 and queue-6 paths are exercised only by a forced test.

**Update (2026-11-16, later) — `data/derived/final/` is no longer in git; counts and checksums of the build.** `data/derived/final/` (about 74 MB) and `data/kaggle_upload/` are in `.gitignore`; the files are rebuilt by `python data/build_final_dataset.py --review-complete --write` and verified against the numbers below (`shasum -a 256 data/derived/final/*.jsonl`). A second build on the same inputs gave byte-identical files (the build is seeded). Inputs: the decisions file as committed at `f62a53f`, the rule files `review_queue_rule_decisions.jsonl` (queue 5) and `review_queue_lineprobe_decisions.jsonl`, and the code at the commit that records this update.

| `val.jsonl` | 679 | `bc678750e0f8270ca3b1915022737371da31ec9ce3278367d6f5807183777745` |

**Update (2026-11-20) — the final test set, and what it must beat.** Built and packaged from the dataset of the 2026-11-16 updates (checksums there): **test 363 rows (170 incorrect, 193 correct)**, validation 679 (277 / 402), train 5,569 (2,366 / 3,203), test_synth 374 (187 / 187). (An earlier message of 2026-11-16 listed train as 2,367 / 3,202: that was the count before the duplicate Class 7 row was dropped.) Test errors per class (natural / induced): 1: 21 / 9, 2: 0 / 4, 3: 14 / 4, 4: 16 / 3, 5: 1 / 19, 6: 1 / 15, 7: 45 / 18. By source (`found_in`): `test_real` 25 errors + 77 correct, queue 1 99 errors, queue 4 14 errors, queue 2 32 errors + 116 correct. The length-only number rose from 0.609 when the 26 errors were removed, so a model has to clear about 0.68 before it can claim more than length. Of the Kaggle notebook, only the load, format and split cells were run locally on the packaged files (6,985 rows; split 5,569 / 679 taken from the rows; `INCLUDE_POLICY = True`); cells 7 and later need a GPU and were not run.

## D-023 — Preparing the final training run (notebooks read the final dataset, new validation, source check, more flips, judge script, packaging)

**Date:** 2026-11-12. **Status:** all of it works on the dry-run preview (`data/derived/final_preview/`, gitignored, written by `build_final_dataset.py --preview`); **no API call, no training, no final file written** (the builder still refuses without `--write --review-complete`; 0 review decisions exist). Steps 1 and 2 share one commit because the notebook patches are intertwined; the others are one commit each.

**1. Notebooks read the final dataset.** `DATASET = "legacy" | "final"` sits at the top of the first code cell of all four notebooks (Colab default `legacy`, Kaggle default `final`). `legacy` is today's behavior (the six files plus `test_real`, 7,580 rows asserted, frozen split); it was re-run on the Kaggle notebooks and gives the same numbers as before (6,816 / 764). `final` reads `train`, `val`, `test`, `test_synth` (`data/derived/final/NAME.jsonl`, or `final_NAME.jsonl` in the Kaggle dataset; `TCV_FINAL_DIR` overrides), takes the split from each row, has no row-count assert, prints counts by split against class, source, origin and domain, and fails only if a split is empty or a test row is synthetic (a duplicate id or a mismatched `split` field is a printed warning). `INCLUDE_POLICY` follows `DATASET` (on in final mode, ModernBERT only; Qwen keeps the v1 input); `INCLUDE_POLICY_OVERRIDE` (None, True, False) forces it, which step 8 needs. The patches are scripts (`notebooks/freeze_split.py`, `add_policy_flag.py`, `add_final_mode.py`, then `make_kaggle_notebooks.py`), each exact-match and idempotent. **Fit, measured with the real tokenizers on the preview (6,693 rows incl. test):** ModernBERT v2 with the policy block: training inputs p50/p90/p99/max 547/3,529/4,069/4,096, validation 364/3,772/4,082/4,096, test 2,317/3,370/4,031/4,053, test_synth 494/3,448/4,059/4,081, and **0 rows over 4,096 after the tightening ladder** in every split; Qwen v1: max 1,497 tokens, 0 over its 1,536 limit. One data fix came out of the smoke test: five CriticTool "next step" rows carried free text as `arguments` (a stringified set), which crashes the notebooks' formatter; the builder now wraps them as `{"text": ...}` and marks `arguments_wrapped`.

**2. Final evaluation report.** In final mode the evaluation cell also prints, for the real test set: AUROC with a 95% bootstrap interval, precision, recall, F1 and false-flag rate at 0.5, recall at 5% and 10% false-alarm rates, recall per class 1 to 7 split by origin (natural, induced), each cell with its count and **"too few to read (k/n)" when n is under 10**, per domain, length-only and source-only baseline AUROC trained on the training split and applied to the same rows; then the synthetic test (`test_synth`, labeled synthetic and not real errors) with the same sections; it writes `test_predictions.jsonl` and `test_synth_predictions.jsonl` with row ids, and the Kaggle notebooks also write everything to `eval_report.txt`. Run with tiny random models (the numbers mean nothing) in a fake Kaggle layout for both notebooks; eval-only without tokenizer files also works in final mode. `eval/judge_new_test.py` prints the same sections for the judges, so the report format is identical.

**3. Validation split.** `assign_val` in the builder is now a seeded simulated-annealing search over whole `(domain, task_id)` groups (60,000 steps; Python and numpy only, so it no longer depends on the scikit-learn version, which removes the D-020 caveat for the final dataset). Targets: about 10% of the training rows, at least 20 airline rows, **at least 20 telecom rows**, at least 15 rows of every error class that has at least 100 rows (a smaller class gets 10% of its rows), and the incorrect share kept. If the minimums cannot all be met together, the domain minimums win: a missing domain row is weighted 30/8 times a missing class row (`DOMAIN_WEIGHT`), and the build prints `MISSED targets: ...` naming each class or domain that fell short. **History (2026-11-12):** the airline minimum was first 30 (validation 674 rows, airline 42, train airline 65), then lowered to 20 (airline train 84), which left telecom validation at 2 rows because the search had no telecom target; the telecom minimum of 20 was then added. Dry-run result: **validation 668 of 6,217 rows (10.7%), airline 23, telecom 61, classes 1 to 7: 22, 57, 10 (target 7 of 74), 69, 15, 79, 16; all minimums met, nothing missed.** Class 5 (15) and Class 7 (16) sit right on their minimum of 15. Telecom overshoots its minimum (61, not 20) because its task groups are large and the search has to take whole groups; the cost is telecom training 422 rows (was 481). Airline is unchanged: train 84, validation 23 (14 correct, 9 incorrect), enough only for a coarse airline number. The incorrect share is 40.1% in validation against 42.0% overall (it was 41.9% before). Train / validation by domain: airline 84/23, bfcl 2,470/313, critictool 732/146, retail 1,841/125, telecom 422/61 (train 5,549, validation 668). Retail validation shrank from 184 to 125 rows. Test rows are untouched.

**4. Source shortcut.** `python eval/shortcut_baselines.py --final` on the preview, validation AUROC of a logistic regression on the coarse source (tau2, BFCL, CriticTool, induced): **everything 0.597, without CriticTool 0.587, without the state flips 0.590, without both 0.560; none is above 0.65, so no fix is proposed and nothing was changed.** (D-021's 0.619 was measured on the old fold with the flips added to 7,580 rows; the new dataset has a different mix and split.) Length alone gives 0.57 to 0.60 on validation and 0.654 on `test_real` (the real errors are long telecom runs). Two cautions: the fine-grained `source` column predicts the label almost perfectly (validation AUROC 0.97 to 1.00, because each synthetic generator source holds one class); it is never part of the model input, but it must stay out of any feature. Incorrect share by coarse source in training: tau2 49.4%, CriticTool 50.0%, BFCL 32.9%; if a future version of the data pushes a coarse source baseline above 0.65, the options are to balance correct rows per source (tau2 has 1,183 correct and 1,154 incorrect, BFCL 1,672 and 818, CriticTool 355 and 355), to reformat CriticTool rows to the tau2 layout, or to drop CriticTool.

**5. More Class 7 flips (airline and telecom only).** Same method and checks as D-021, rules from `airline/policy.md` and `telecom/main_policy.md` only, none chosen from test rows. The 194 original rows are reproduced exactly; the generator now also writes one more negative for every other applicable rule of a source row (a row with two applicable rules used to give one flip). Result: **203 prohibition-rule rows (164 retail, 25 airline, 14 telecom; retail is 81%, down from 85%)**: added per rule, telecom resume with an expired contract +4 (3 to 7), telecom resume with an unpaid overdue bill +3 (4 to 7), airline modify flights on basic economy +1 (2 to 3), airline payment method not in the profile for a flight change +1 (6 to 7). The pair check passes for all 237 rows, and the baselines on the pairs are as before (length 0.475, source 0.500 on 406 pair rows). **Also written, not merged:** `class7_stateflip_conditional.jsonl`, 34 telecom rows for two rules phrased as instructions rather than prohibitions ("if the line is not roaming enabled, enable it": 32 rows with roaming already on; "lift the suspension" on a line that is not suspended: 2 rows); enabling roaming that is already on is harmless, so I did not count them as policy violations. **Rules I looked for and could not use:** refuel (no plan lookup in any history, so the data limit is unknown; the 2 GB maximum is an argument rule); cancel_reservation (0 of 5: three have a flown segment, two a stated reason that could allow it); passenger count and bag removal (no train-split candidate of those tools); the flight-availability rule (only 2 of 24 booking rows looked up a flight status, and the search results would contradict an edited status); send_certificate (5 rows, each with up to 9 reservation lookups, so the compensation conditions would need edits in many places); "already flown" for a cabin change (editing a flight date makes the unchanged call inconsistent with the reservation). So airline and telecom stay small: 39 rows, and the flips remain mostly a retail signal.

**6. Judge baseline (`eval/judge_new_test.py`, not run).** Phase 0 prompt unchanged, gpt-5-nano and gpt-5-mini at low reasoning effort. Default mode only estimates; `--run` calls the API. Cost: input tokens from the prompt length (3.38 characters per token and 229 / 195 output tokens per call, both calibrated on the recorded Phase 0 runs), refuses to start above `--cap-usd` (default 2.50; `--force` overrides), stops when real usage reaches the cap and keeps what is done. Cache: `results/judge_new_test/<model>.jsonl` keyed by trajectory id and a hash of the exact prompt; the 102 `test_real` rows reuse the Phase 0 answers (same prompt; `--no-phase0-cache` turns that off). `--report-only` reproduces the Phase 0 numbers from the cache without any call: nano AUROC 0.709, mini 0.667 on the 102 rows, as in D-006. **Expected cost on the dry-run test (102 rows, all cached): $0.00;** with the projected review additions (up to 149 error candidates and the 150 correct examples of queue 2) about **$0.35 if 40% of the candidates are accepted and $0.50 if all are, for both judges** (about 312 to 401 rows); `--with-synth` would add the 374 synthetic rows. `--compare test_predictions.jsonl` prints our model's AUROC on the same rows.

**7. Kaggle package (`data/package_for_kaggle.py`).** Copies `final_train.jsonl`, `final_val.jsonl`, `final_test.jsonl` and `final_test_synth.jsonl` from `data/derived/final/` into `data/kaggle_upload/` (gitignored), re-checks the two conditions the notebooks fail on, prints names, sizes and row counts, and refuses if the final dataset has not been written (`--preview` packages the dry run and marks it; `--with-legacy` adds the 7 old files). On the preview: about 71 MB in total (train 58 MB).

**8. Scoring today's v2 checkpoint on the new test set (the steps are in `docs/kaggle_runbook.md`).** Attach as an input the output of the Kaggle notebook version that trained v2 (its files appear as `/kaggle/input/<notebook-slug>/checkpoints/phase5_v2/best/`; if v2 came from Colab, upload that Drive folder as a dataset), plus the new data version; set `DATASET = "final"`, `EVAL_ONLY = True`, `EVAL_CHECKPOINT` to that `best/` path and **`INCLUDE_POLICY_OVERRIDE = False`**, because v2 was trained without the policy block and a block it never saw would measure the format, not the data. **Two contaminations to handle:** (1) the old training data contained 1,309 rows of 37 tasks that are now test tasks (D-018), so the old model has seen those tasks; `eval/score_old_model_on_new_test.py test_predictions.jsonl` separates CLEAN rows (the 74 `test_real` tasks plus new test tasks not in the overlap list) from OVERLAPPING rows, and only the clean line isolates what the new data adds; (2) all of `test_synth` comes from task groups that were in the old training data, so it is not a held-out result for the old model. The old-model run and the new-data run should be compared on the same clean rows.

**Not verified:** anything on Kaggle or on a real GPU (D-020 still applies); the final test set (it has 102 rows until the review adds more, so every per-class cell is "too few to read" today); the judge script against the API (only the cache path and the estimate ran).

## D-024 — Labeling guide, Class 7 renamed "Policy violation", queue 5, and queue-2 errors in the test set (2026-11-15)

**Why.** Queue 2 (150 correct-example checks) came back with 66 "wrong" of 148 decided, and 50 of those 66 were Class 7. 37 of the 66 are read-only calls (`get_*`, `search_*`, `check_*` ...), many issued in parallel with other lookups. The class definition ("missing precondition / wrong order") had been stretched to cover "the agent did several lookups at once" and "the agent acted without a yes", which are different things. `docs/labeling_guide.md` now fixes the definitions.

**Decisions.**
1. **Class 7 is "Policy violation"** (the call performs or starts an action the domain policy does not allow at that point). The numeric id is unchanged. Renamed in the README, `docs/failure-taxonomy.md`, `data/taxonomy.md`, the display names of the review tools, the projection and the builder (`error_class_name` of Class 7 rows becomes `policy_violation`). Not renamed: dated entries of this file (they describe what was known then), the stored slug `missing_precondition_wrong_order` in older data files, and the prompts of the labelers already run (changing them would change what the cached labels mean).
2. **Sub-reasons** `no_confirmation`, `ineligible_state`, `missing_step`, `precondition_error`, `parallel_write`, `other` (with a note), one per row, with a precedence order in the guide. Stored as `class7_reason` with `class7_reason_source` = `human` (a queue-5 decision), `generator` (the state-flip rows are `ineligible_state` by construction) or `heuristic` (`data/class7_reasons.py`, structure only, `unlabeled` when it finds nothing).
3. **A read-only call is never Class 7** unless the policy forbids that specific lookup. **Parallel read-only lookups are correct**, tag `parallel_lookup`. This departs on purpose from the tau2-bench policies' "one tool call at a time" rule (the guide says why: the rule protects writes, independent lookups cannot hurt). A write issued while another call of the same message is open is `parallel_write`. Read-only means the prefixes the guide lists plus the telecom diagnostics `check_*`, `can_*`, `run_speed_test`, which read the device and change nothing; that last part is my extension of the stated list and is easy to remove (`data/class7_reasons.py`).
4. **Queue 5** (`data/review_queues.py add-queue5`, 92 items, about 46 minutes): every queue-2 "wrong" on a read-only call (37, whatever class it was given), every Class 7 decision of queues 1 and 2 on a write call (54) and, because the rule makes it suspect, the one queue-1 Class 7 decision on a read-only call (`can_send_mms`). Each item shows the rule box, the domain policy file, the reviewer's original decision, the call, and a heuristic pre-fill with the signals that produced it (calls sharing an assistant message; no affirmative user message before a write; the order or reservation status in the latest lookup; an id that no earlier result contains). Keys: `k` keep the pre-fill, `n e m p w o` a Class 7 reason, `y` correct, `l` correct + `parallel_lookup`, `1-6` another class, `s` skip (the original stays), `u`, `t`, `f`, `g`, `q`. Original decisions are never edited: queue-5 records are keyed `q5::<original id>` and `data/review_decisions.py` applies them over the originals for the builder, the projection and `merge`. Pre-fill proposals on the 92 items: 26 `correct + parallel_lookup`, 11 `correct`, 1 Class 4, 18 `ineligible_state`, 7 `no_confirmation`, 4 `missing_step`, 3 `parallel_write`, 22 none. **Not verified:** the pre-fill's accuracy (no human decision exists yet); it never reads the policy.
5. **Queue-2 errors count as test errors.** `build_final_dataset.py` adds every queue-2 item a human marked wrong with a class as an incorrect test row (`found_in: "queue2"`, `source: queue2_found_error`, `origin` kept), only in the test split, with the same split rules (the task groups are test tasks already; the straddle check still runs). A queue-2 error that is also in queue 5 and has no queue-5 decision yet is **left out until re-reviewed** (53 of the 66 today); once queue 5 decides it, the queue-5 verdict is the one used. Every test row now has `found_in` (`test_real`, `queue1`, `queue2`, `queue4`).
6. **Duplicates.** Two test rows are the same call when they share the run and the call index (test_real rows, which have no index, by run, tool and arguments). **None found.** A first version keyed on run + tool + arguments reported 5 "duplicates", all of them an agent legitimately repeating an identical call (the Class 4 pattern: a call and its repeat), so it was replaced.
7. **Class 7 recall by sub-reason** is added to the final-mode report of the two notebooks (`notebooks/add_class7_report.py`, applied to the Colab notebooks and regenerated into the Kaggle ones): human-confirmed reasons first, heuristic ones listed apart and marked as not human labels. Checked only on made-up rows, not on a model run.

**Caveats.** (1) The train and validation Class 7 rows from the review pool have heuristic reasons only. The 8 Class 7 rows of `test_real`, labeled before this guide, were added to the end of queue 5 afterwards (see the update below). (2) Queue-4 Class 7 decisions (none yet) are not in queue 5; `add-queue5 --rebuild` after queue 4 would add them. (3) Until queue 5 is done the Class 7 test count (47 today) excludes the 50 queue-2 Class 7 errors held for it.

**Update (2026-11-15) — the 8 Class 7 rows of `test_real.jsonl` are in queue 5.** `review_queues.py add-queue5-testreal` appended them as items 93 to 100 (the 92 existing lines and their order are unchanged; `add-queue5 --rebuild` also puts them at the end). They have no earlier decision record, so each item carries a display copy built from the raw run (the call is found in the run by tool and arguments, unique for all 8: 5 telecom `resume_line`, airline `send_certificate`, `book_reservation`, `update_reservation_flights`) and shows the phase-4 hand label as the original decision. The same screen, rule box and pre-fill apply; the pre-fill finds a signal for 1 of the 8 (the basic-economy flight change) and none for the telecom `resume_line` rows, which need the policy's SIM and contract rules. **A queue-5 decision replaces the phase-4 label in the builder and the projection** (`apply_queue5_to_test_real`, `review_decisions.effective`): a Class 7 verdict adds the reason and `class7_reason_source: human`, another class relabels the row, `correct` turns it into a correct example; skipped or undecided rows keep the phase-4 label. Checked on a scratch decisions file (one row kept as Class 7, one made correct, one made Class 1: test errors 148 to 146, correct examples 159 to 160); the real decisions file is untouched and queue 5 has 100 items (about 50 minutes).

**Update (2026-11-15, later) — queue 5 is no longer reviewed by hand; the rules of the D-022 update of the same date apply it. The queue and its screen remain, and a human decision overrides the rule.**

**Update (2026-11-20) — judge baseline on the final test set, and the runbook.** `eval/judge_new_test.py` with the Phase 0 prompt on the 363 test rows (the 102 `test_real` rows reuse Phase 0 answers): **gpt-5-mini AUROC 0.719, 95% bootstrap interval 0.665-0.771**; precision 0.769, recall 0.547, F1 0.639, false-flag rate 0.145; recall 0.100 at 5% and 0.471 at 10% false alarms (the scores are discrete, so these two points are coarse). **gpt-5-nano AUROC 0.619, interval 0.561-0.678**; precision 0.638, recall 0.435, F1 0.517, false-flag rate 0.218; recall 0.112 at 5% and 0.229 at 10%. Next to them: length only 0.675, source only 0.422. So gpt-5-nano is below the length shortcut and gpt-5-mini is above it by 0.044 with overlapping uncertainty (the intervals of the judge and of a bootstrap of the length baseline were not compared).

Recall at the verdict flag per class (natural / induced), gpt-5-mini then gpt-5-nano, cells under 10 rows omitted: Class 1 0.76 / (7 of 9) and 0.52 / (5 of 9); Class 3 0.64 / (3 of 4) and 0.79 / (1 of 4); Class 4 0.56 / (3 of 3) and 0.25 / (3 of 3); Class 5 (1 of 1) / 0.53 and (1 of 1) / 0.47; Class 6 (0 of 1) / 0.53 and (0 of 1) / 0.27; Class 7 0.38 / 0.33 (17 of 45, 6 of 18) for mini and 0.33 / 0.39 (15 of 45, 7 of 18) for nano; Class 2 (4 of 4 and 3 of 4, induced only). **Class 7 overall: mini 23 of 63 (0.37), nano 22 of 63 (0.35).** Per domain (AUROC; recall at the verdict, false-flag): mini airline 0.699, 0.55 (29/53), 0.17; retail 0.710, 0.43 (31/72), 0.09; telecom 0.734, 0.73 (33/45), 0.25. nano airline 0.546, 0.36 (19/53), 0.22; retail 0.644, 0.44 (32/72), 0.19; telecom 0.635, 0.51 (23/45), 0.28. On `test_synth` (nano only): AUROC 0.874 (0.838-0.910), recall 0.850, false-flag 0.267; Class 2 recall 0.93 (135/145), Class 6 0.57 (24/42); the synthetic rows are far easier than the real ones, and gpt-5-mini was not run on them. **Cost:** $0.347 for both judges on the test set and $0.078 for nano on `test_synth`, $0.425 in all against the $0.80 cap (running mini on `test_synth` as well would have cost about $0.42 more and passed the cap, so it was left out); `api_spend.json` totals $4.95 of the $6.14 limit. Predictions with row ids are in `results/judge_new_test/<model>_test_predictions.jsonl` (and `_test_synth_`), the raw answers in `results/judge_new_test/<model>.jsonl`. **Caveats:** the judges see a conversation window and the Phase 0 prompt, not the policy text per rule; the Class 7 sub-reasons used in the report are heuristic; a bootstrap over 363 rows leaves per-class and per-domain cells wide.

`data/kaggle_upload/` (4 files, 74.3 MB) matches the checksums of D-022. `docs/kaggle_runbook.md` now gives the exact steps for (a) uploading the files as a new version of `toolcall-verifier-data`, (b) the 100-step benchmark (flags and the output to send back), (c) the final ModernBERT run with Save & Run All (`DATASET = "final"`, `INCLUDE_POLICY` on, one GPU) and (d) the eval-only run of the v2 checkpoint (`INCLUDE_POLICY_OVERRIDE = False`; the CLEAN rows are 242 of the 363: 104 incorrect, 138 correct). **Not verified:** anything on Kaggle or on a GPU; the benchmark, the step times and whether the final run fits the time budget are still unmeasured.

## D-025 — Diagnosis of the first final ModernBERT run (final_v1) and the next experiment (2026-11-21)

(Numbered D-025 because D-024 is the labeling-guide entry.) No training setting was changed, no API call and no Kaggle run was made for this entry. Scripts: `eval/diagnose_final_v1.py` (output in `results/final_v1/diagnosis.txt`) and `eval/diagnose_visibility.py` (`results/final_v1/visibility.txt`).

**Files.** From `~/Downloads` (the newest of each; the earlier `eval_report.txt`, `train_info.txt`, `test_real_predictions.txt` are the Nov 12 v2 run): `eval_report2.txt` (Nov 21 11:50) to `docs/final_v1_eval_report.txt`, `train_info2.txt` (11:52) to `docs/final_v1_train_info.json`, `test_predictions.txt` (11:52, 363 rows) to `checkpoints/final_v1/test_predictions.jsonl` (also `results/final_v1/test_predictions.jsonl`, because `checkpoints/` is not tracked by git). **The executed notebook is not in `~/Downloads`** (the newest notebook there is `phase5-modernbert.ipynb` of Nov 12, the earlier run), so `docs/final_v1_run.ipynb` was not written; download the Kaggle notebook version and tell me where it is. The predictions file has only `trajectory_id, label, error_class, domain, p_incorrect` (no origin or source); this is a notebook bug found here: the last lines of the evaluation cell overwrote the richer file with the legacy format. Origins were joined back from the test file by id.

**The run.** One T4, micro-batch 2 x accumulation 8, no gradient checkpointing, 5,569 train rows, 3 epochs, 1,047 steps, **4,338 s (1.2 h)** (the speed fix of D-020 worked: the first Kaggle run took 7.75 h). Validation AUROC 0.726, 0.863, 0.895; best epoch 3 (the checkpoint is chosen by validation F1, 0.240, 0.702, 0.711, which here is also the last epoch). The logged training loss looks 8 times too large (5.5 at the start is about 8 x ln 2: the sum over the accumulation steps); the validation loss is 0.397. Test (363 rows): AUROC 0.667 (0.614-0.723), precision 0.677, recall 0.259, false-flag rate 0.109; recall 0.100 at 5% and 0.218 at 10% false alarms (thresholds 0.550 and 0.514); Class 7 recall 0.22 (14/63); test_synth AUROC 0.739, synthetic Class 2 recall 0.34 (50/145), Class 6 1.00 (42/42). Bars: length-only 0.675, gpt-5-mini 0.719, gpt-5-nano 0.619.

**1. Training composition.** Of 2,366 training errors **16 are real (0.7%): 10 natural Class 7 and 6 natural Class 1; no induced error is in training** (the pair rule held the induced errors back, D-022). The rest: tau2 synthetic 1,023, BFCL 804, CriticTool 363 (all Class 6), state flips 160 (all Class 7, retail). Per class (real / synthetic or external): Class 1 6 / 183; Class 2 0 / 613; Class 3 0 / 64; Class 4 0 / 644; Class 5 0 / 197; Class 6 0 / 489; Class 7 10 / 160. The correct rows: 1,275 synthetic controls and 1,928 real correct calls (601 tau2 gold, BFCL gold, CriticTool correct). 57% of the training rows (BFCL 2,432 and CriticTool 726) are in no test row; the tau2 domains are 2,411 rows (airline only 100). Validation has 15 real errors (13 Class 7, 2 Class 1) of 277.

**2. Validation by source.** The run did not save validation predictions, so **the model's validation AUROC per source cannot be computed from the files we have**; only the overall 0.895 exists, and 262 of the 277 validation errors are synthetic or external, so it measures synthetic detection. What can be said: counts per source (BFCL 351 rows, 107 errors; CriticTool 152, 76; tau2 synthetic 149, 67; state flips 12 errors; natural 15; induced 0) and the length-only baseline per source, which is at chance on every synthetic source (BFCL 0.505, CriticTool 0.503, tau2 synthetic 0.518; state flips 0.626; natural errors 0.315, i.e. shorter than the correct rows; 15 rows). Within a domain length does not separate the labels in training (AUROC 0.50-0.53) or validation, because the generators matched lengths with paired controls. The notebook now saves `val_predictions.jsonl` (see below), so the next run can report this table.

**3. Score distribution on test.** Median score 0.314 for correct rows (natural 0.301, induced 0.440) and 0.424 for incorrect rows; all scores lie between about 0.09 and 0.58 (5th to 95th percentile), 6 rows reach 0.7. AUROC of each group against all correct rows (95% interval where n >= 10): Class 1 0.672 (0.571-0.766), Class 2 0.870 (n = 4), Class 3 0.550 (0.396-0.693), Class 4 0.649 (0.511-0.770), Class 5 0.705 (0.584-0.816), Class 6 0.768 (0.662-0.859), Class 7 0.653 (0.577-0.726). By origin: natural 0.616 (0.547-0.689), induced 0.737 (0.675-0.801); natural Class 3 is at chance (0.479, 14 rows), natural Class 7 0.630, natural Class 1 0.609, natural Class 4 0.667; induced Class 6 0.760, Class 7 0.711, Class 5 0.692. **Some separation exists for Classes 6 and 5 and the induced rows; none for Class 3.** By domain: airline 0.749 (length-only 0.698), retail 0.687 (0.733), telecom 0.599 (0.629): the model beats length only in airline. **The score tracks length**: Spearman +0.55 with the input length (+0.46 to +0.59 within each domain), +0.55 within correct rows alone. Within thirds of the length distribution the model's AUROC is 0.519 (shortest third, 28% errors), 0.637 and 0.655, so there is some signal beyond length in the middle and long thirds and none in the shortest. **Part of the length effect is the test construction:** the natural correct examples come from before the first flagged call (median 16 turns of history) and the natural errors from the flagged call (median 20), so the raw number of turns alone gives AUROC 0.626 (natural 0.627) and the length-only baseline 0.675. Why the model's scores follow length when length is uninformative inside training domains is not explained by these files.

**4. Visibility.** The policy block is on 199 of 363 test rows (55%) and adds a median 166 tokens (max 334). The ladder fired (natural length above 4,096 tokens) on 26 test rows with the policy against 20 without; the visible share of tool-result characters is 68.9% pooled with the policy and 69.4% without (mean 84.0% against 84.2%); 13 rows moved one or more ladder levels further, 8 lost some evidence (mean 9.1% of it, at most 37%). On the 102 `test_real` rows, the earlier v2 run's inputs, the ladder fired on 5 rows either way. Class by class the visible share is identical to the decimal except Class 7 (86.6% against 87.3%). In training the ladder fired on 383 rows with the policy and 300 without. **The policy text does not crowd out evidence in any way that matters;** but even without it, 155 of the 337 test rows that have tool results lose some of them (mean visible 84%), the same as in v2.

**5. Old v2 versus final.** On the 102 `test_real` rows of the new test set (25 incorrect, same labels in both files): v2 AUROC 0.686 (0.538-0.831), final 0.688 (0.556-0.809), **difference +0.003, paired-bootstrap 95% interval -0.116 to +0.115**. Class 4 got worse (recall at 0.5 from 1.00 to 0.57, median score 0.93 to 0.52, 7 rows), Class 7 better (0.12 to 0.62, 8 rows), Class 1 0.33 to 0.17 (6 rows); the correct rows' median score rose (0.36 to 0.40). The two models' scores correlate at +0.66. **A new, human-reviewed, much larger synthetic training set, the policy text and a 4,096-token input changed nothing measurable on the real rows.**

**Diagnosis.** (i) The model learned the synthetic corpora (validation 0.895) and little that transfers: 99.3% of its errors are synthetic, none induced, Classes 2 to 6 have no real example. (ii) On real rows it is statistically the same as the old model (+0.003) and as length (0.667 against 0.675). (iii) The test set carries a length artifact that any model can use, so 0.675 is not a floor for "understanding". (iv) The policy block is not the cause. (v) More synthetic data of the same kind is unlikely to help; the missing ingredient is real examples, which today exist only in the test set (363 rows, 170 errors, 118 task groups).

**What cannot be done with the current data.** *Weighting or oversampling real rows:* only 16 real errors (10 Class 7, 6 Class 1) and no induced error are in training, so it would repeat 16 rows. *Best checkpoint by validation AUROC on real rows only:* 15 real validation errors against 52 real correct (tau2 gold), all of Classes 7 and 1; the interval of such an AUROC is about +-0.15, so it cannot choose between checkpoints (and here the best epoch is already the last). *Train only on real and induced rows plus matching correct calls:* 16 real errors; impossible without using the test rows.

**Experiments, at most three, one change each, one Kaggle run of about 1.5 hours each.**
1. **REAL_CV (recommended; implemented, default off).** Fine-tune the final_v1 checkpoint on the human-reviewed real rows with grouped 5-fold cross-validation over the 363 test rows (118 task groups, folds of 72-73 rows and 34 errors, whole groups, no leakage), and score every row with a model that never saw its group. One change: real rows enter training. It runs from the existing checkpoint (`EVAL_ONLY = True`) and takes about 30 minutes (5 folds x about 290 rows x 3 epochs at lr 1e-5, plus scoring). **Reason:** every diagnosis above says the limit is real signal, and this is the only way to test that without new labeling. **Expected effect (a judgment, not a measurement):** if real errors are learnable at all, out-of-fold AUROC should clear the shortcut baselines on the same folds by a clear margin (length only 0.667, length + domain 0.674; a result above about 0.72 would be evidence); if it stays within about 0.03 of 0.67 then fine-tuning on 290 real rows does not help and the limit is the input or the model. The run prints those baselines next to the result. **Caveat:** the 363 rows stop being an untouched held-out set; the out-of-fold number is a cross-validated estimate on the same rows, and a model finally trained on all of them has no held-out test until new real rows are collected.
2. **Domain-restricted training (tau2 domains only, optimizer steps held equal).** Drop BFCL and CriticTool (57% of the rows, in no test row), keep the 2,411 tau2-domain rows and train about 7 epochs so that the number of steps stays about 1,047 (about 1.2 h, the same as final_v1). Reason: the capacity and the steps go to the domain of the test; it is a flag away (a domain filter) but is not implemented yet. Expected effect: small, perhaps a few points on retail and telecom; it cannot create the missing real signal.
3. **Four epochs on the same data (about 1.6 h).** Reason: validation AUROC was still rising (0.726, 0.863, 0.895) when the learning rate reached zero. Expected effect: validation up, test within about +-0.02, because validation is synthetic. Not recommended before experiment 1. Policy off was considered and dropped: the visibility check shows the policy costs almost nothing, so an ablation would only measure noise.

**Implemented for the recommended experiment** (`notebooks/real_cv_code.py`, applied by `make_kaggle_notebooks.py` to `kaggle_phase5_modernbert.ipynb`): flags `REAL_CV = False`, `REAL_CV_FOLDS = 5`, `REAL_CV_EPOCHS = 3`, `REAL_CV_LR = 1e-5` in the configuration cell and a last cell that, when on, builds the folds, fine-tunes per fold from the evaluated checkpoint, and prints the pooled out-of-fold AUROC with a 95% interval, the plain-checkpoint AUROC on the same rows, the paired difference, recall at 5% and 10% false alarms, recall per class and origin, the per-domain AUROC and the out-of-fold shortcut baselines (length; length + domain); it saves `real_cv_predictions.jsonl` with the fold of each row. Two reporting fixes ride along (neither changes training): the prediction file is no longer overwritten in final mode, and `val_predictions.jsonl` is saved. **Smoke test:** the whole notebook ran on a laptop CPU (`TCV_SMOKE=1`, random toy model, 60 test rows) with the flag off (the cell reports it is skipped), on (5 folds, report, predictions) and in `EVAL_ONLY` mode from a saved checkpoint; the numbers of such a run are meaningless, only the code path was checked. **Not verified:** the run on a real checkpoint and GPU (the time estimate is from the measured 4 rows per second of final_v1), and whether fp16 and the micro-batch 2 fit with the longest 4,096-token rows (the fold fine-tuning falls back to micro-batch 1 with checkpointing after an out-of-memory error, which was exercised only by reading the code).

## D-026 — Real data in training: the data fix after the first final run (2026-11-21)

No API call; the test files are byte-identical. Goal: the D-025 diagnosis found 16 real errors among 2,366 training errors.

**Why there were 16 real errors and 0 induced.** (1) The natural gate required the second labeler (gpt-5-nano) to agree with the first (gpt-5-mini); 163 train-split candidates were `auto_rejected` by that disagreement, and natural Classes 3, 5, 6 were out by decision (D-022). (2) **Induced errors need a paired correct handling** (the pair rule of the builder), and the only way a train-split positive was admitted was `--train-positives gated`, which needs the human-confirmed share of that origin's queue-2 examples to be at least 90%; for induced rows that share is 22 of 48 (46%), so no induced positive was admitted and the pair rule dropped all 12 induced Class 5 train errors (and the one Class 6). (3) The natural positives (993) were never admitted either, because `accepted` mode needs a human decision on a train-split row and queue 2 only drew from the test split. The fix is in `data/build_final_dataset.py`.

**What the builder now does (defaults; `--train-positives linked`).**
- **Natural Classes 1, 4, 7** enter on the first labeler's label alone (`TRAIN_FIRST_LABELER`; tier `first_labeler_gated`, field `second_labeler_agreed`). Natural 3, 5, 6 stay out. Human-measured first-labeler precision on the test split: 100%, 73%, 68% (before the line-probe and read-only rules).
- **A deviation from the literal instruction, with the reason:** the labeling-guide rules are applied to those errors too (`screen_candidate`; `--no-error-rules` switches it off), because the test set already follows them and training on the opposite labels would contradict it: a read-only call proposed as Class 7 is not an error (23 candidates removed), and a first lookup of a customer's line that the line-probe rule judges correct is not an error (15, all natural Class 1, telecom `get_details_by_id` on `L1001`). **Without the rules the builder admits natural 29 / 32 / 95 errors of Classes 1 / 4 / 7; with them 14 / 32 / 72.**
- **Pending positives** (the earlier calls of the same run, `linked_candidate_id`) of every first-labeler candidate enter as correct rows after the rules (`screen_positive`): 748 natural admitted; 14 excluded as an identical repeat of an earlier call (Class 4), 3 as a lookup of a different line than the known target, 1 for a structural Class 7 signal. The positives of an excluded error stay in (they are calls before the flagged call; only the flagged call was judged not an error). Measured on queue 2 after the rules, natural positives are 93 of 98 correct (94.9%); the screen would have caught only 1 of the 5 wrong ones.
- **Induced Class 5** (`induced_propagation`): 12 auto-accepted train errors (tier `auto_accepted_gated`; 19 of 19 confirmed on the test split) with their paired correct handlings, linked by run. Only 6 of the 11 linked positives are admitted (5 carry a structural Class 7 signal). **Warning: the induced paired positives are the weak side.** In queue 2 the induced `prop` positives were confirmed correct only 6 of 12 times (the wrong ones are real later mistakes of Classes 1, 3, 6 and 7); the 6 admitted rows therefore carry about 50% label noise. 10 further induced Class 5 train candidates are held back (`needs_human_review`, flag `user_repeated_old_value`: the user repeated the old value, so using it may be right). Induced Class 2 stays out (3 of 16 confirmed).
- **Induced Class 6:** there is no soft-fault pair in the training split (all 25 soft-fault candidates are test rows); the one train Class 6 candidate is a hard fault (`induced_fault`), outside the instruction, and is held back; `strict_check.py` was therefore not needed on any train row.
- **Duplicates in the training pool.** The checker found 13 groups of identical content inside train (same turns and same call, from different trials of a task, or a repeat). `dedupe_content` now keeps one row of a group with one label (a real-origin row first) and drops every row of a group whose labels conflict (3 groups: a correct and an incorrect row, or two classes): 34 rows removed in all (18 + 3 correct duplicates, 6 correct rows equal to a test row, 2 + 2 + 2 rows of the conflicting groups). Test rows are never touched.

**Result (`python eval/train_composition.py`, `results/final_v1/train_composition_before_after.txt`).** Real-origin means natural or induced: errors from real agent runs and their paired correct rows (field `real_origin`, set on train rows only).

| training split | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| rows | 5,569 | 6,231 |
| errors | 2,366 | 2,444 |
| real errors (natural + induced) | 16 (0.7%) | **94 (3.8%)**: 86 natural, 8 induced |
| real correct rows | 0 | **580** (575 natural, 5 induced) |
| real-origin rows | 16 (0.3%) | **674 (10.8%)** |

Real errors per class (natural / induced / synthetic or external, after): Class 1 10 / 0 / 184; Class 2 0 / 0 / 609; Class 3 0 / 0 / 66; Class 4 22 / 0 / 632; Class 5 0 / 8 / 206; Class 6 0 / 0 / 490; Class 7 54 / 0 / 163. (Before: Class 1 6 / 0 / 183, Class 7 10 / 0 / 160, all others 0 real.) Of the 118 natural and 12 induced errors admitted, 13 were lost because their task group was moved to the held-out synthetic test (`test_synth` must not change) and 17 natural and 3 induced errors went to validation. **Validation (749 rows, 281 errors; before 679 and 277): real-origin 92 rows, 20 errors (17 natural, 3 induced) and 72 correct; before 15 real errors and no real correct row.** Class 2, 3 and 6 still have no real training error. The data is skewed to telecom and airline Class 7 and Class 4.

**Verification.** `test.jsonl` and `test_synth.jsonl` have the same SHA-256 as the first final run (`f1c20d8e...fdee6`, `fb2b869b...d779`). New files: `train.jsonl` 6,231 rows `391bd34957e5619d9db851fce862d09f9211f7c7fa26e3ca8e94a8bbd980c39f`; `val.jsonl` 749 rows `1ebfb5d92aef67bd6e69a14e5eab8414906242966a2537e385559f1b558fbb56` (a rebuild with the same inputs is byte-identical). `data/check_final_dataset.py`: ALL CHECKS PASSED (no leakage, no duplicate content, unique ids). **Baselines now:** length only validation 0.584, test 0.675 (unchanged); source only validation 0.569 and **test 0.578 (was 0.422)**: the induced source appears in training for the first time and 77% of the induced test rows are errors, so a source shortcut now carries a little signal; it is small, but a model can use it.

**Notebook (`notebooks/real_weight_code.py`, default off).** `REAL_WEIGHT = 1` in the configuration cell: with an integer k, each real-origin training row appears k times per epoch (the same as sampling it k times more often) and the rest once; with 3 the epoch has 7,579 rows (6,231 + 2 x 674), about 36% longer than before; `train_info.json` records `real_weight`, `n_train_unique` and `n_real_origin_train`. The evaluation cell saves `val_predictions.jsonl` with `source`, `origin` and `real_origin` and prints the validation AUROC per source group (real-origin natural and induced, CriticTool, BFCL, tau2 synthetic) and the **real-origin validation AUROC** with an interval (92 rows, 20 errors: wide). Smoke-tested on CPU (toy model): weight 3 gave 288 rows per epoch from 192, weight 1 no change, the report ran. The checkpoint is still chosen by overall validation F1; the real-origin AUROC is only reported. **Not verified:** a GPU run, and whether weighting helps.

**Package and next run.** `data/kaggle_upload/` is rebuilt (6,231 / 749 / 363 / 374 rows, 79.4 MB; the notebook's data cells ran on it: 7,717 rows, split 6,231 / 749). `docs/kaggle_runbook.md` section (f): `DATASET = "final"`, `REAL_WEIGHT = 3`, 3 epochs, one GPU, `REAL_CV = False`; expected about 1.7 hours (22,737 samples at the 3.85 samples per second of final_v1).

**Cost estimate for localizing the remaining train-split failed runs with gpt-5-mini (not run).** The index holds 1,200 failed runs, 823 in train-split tasks; 292 of those are localized (airline 92 of 92, retail 97 of 190, telecom 103 of 541), so **531 remain: 93 retail and 438 telecom** (none flagged by the old heuristics). Cost: the prompt of the remaining runs measured with the stand-in tokenizer (2.97 M tokens, scaled by 0.912, the ratio to the real usage on 60 localized runs) plus the mean 667 / 473 output tokens per run (retail / telecom) at $0.25 and $2.00 per million: **retail $0.26, telecom $1.02, total about $1.28** (the 668 localizations so far cost $1.74 in all). That is inside your $1.95 balance (leaving about $0.67) but above the $1.19 left under the tracked hard limit of `api_spend.json` ($6.14), so it would need that limit raised or about 85% of the runs. **Expected yield**, from the unflagged localized runs: retail 31% of runs give a Class 1, 4 or 7 candidate (2 / 5 / 22 per 93 runs), telecom 69% (83 / 92 / 129 per 438), about 330 first-labeler candidates. The rules then keep retail Class 7 at 82%, telecom Class 7 at 52% and telecom Class 1 at 25% (the line probes); Class 4 stays whole: **about 205 usable errors** (retail about 25, telecom about 180) and, at 6.2 and 3.7 positives per candidate, **about 820 real correct rows**; about 73% of them would land in training (the rest in validation or lost to the held-out groups). That is about $0.006 per usable error and $0.0012 per real-origin row; telecom is 4 times cheaper per error than retail ($0.0057 against $0.0105). Expected label quality: the 73% and 68% first-labeler precisions of Classes 4 and 7 (so roughly a quarter to a third of the Class 4 and 7 errors would have the wrong class or not be errors). It would add nothing for Classes 2, 3, 5 and 6, and it skews the real data further to telecom Classes 4 and 7.

## D-027 — Analysis of the real-weighted run (final_v2: `REAL_WEIGHT = 3`, 94 real training errors) (2026-11-21)

No API call and no Kaggle run. The notebook is `kaggle-phase5-modernbert-ipynb`, Version 2 ("AUROC 0.732").

**Step 0: files.** The newest files in `~/Downloads` (all of Nov 21, 16:11 to 16:49) were used: `eval_report3.txt` to `docs/final_v2_eval_report.txt`; `test_predictions3.txt`, `test_synth_predictions3.txt` and `val_predictions.txt` to `results/final_v2/{test_predictions,test_synth_predictions,val_predictions}.jsonl` (363, 374 and 749 rows); `model.safetensors`, `config.txt` (a JSON file, saved as `config.json`) and `tokenizer.txt` (JSON, saved as `tokenizer.json`) to `models/final_v2/`. **Not in `~/Downloads`:** `tokenizer_config.json`, a special-tokens file and `train_info.json` of this run (the model loads without them: the saved `tokenizer.json` gives the same token ids as the base tokenizer). `models/` is in `.gitignore`. Files in `models/final_v2/` (SHA-256, bytes):

| file | bytes | SHA-256 |
|---|---|---|
| `model.safetensors` | 598,439,784 | `799c14776fbdffe531e1bd1930ca040f93d2eb2dcf4e921eeb73eeb382b253ef` |
| `config.json` | 2,108 | `538d50966465c05bc290ec4cd4d0b2c5d6d18fc9a6055afb63c7b491517b558e` |
| `tokenizer.json` | 3,583,327 | `0fc88ec706f80b2eb15174d04fa7377948ca3f07065985deec1e28d3fb1cda9d` |

**Local check (`eval/verify_model_locally.py`, CPU, torch 2.15, transformers 5.23).** The model loads as `ModernBertForSequenceClassification` (150M parameters, labels correct / incorrect). On 10 test rows (the 6 shortest inputs, 27 to 202 tokens, and 4 of 1,786 to 2,164 tokens) its fp32 CPU scores differ from the saved GPU fp16 predictions by at most 0.0008 (mean 0.0003). The inputs were built with the notebook's own formatting cells (policy block on). **The run's headline numbers are those of its evaluation report:** test AUROC 0.732 (0.678-0.781); without Class 7 0.708; recall 0.688 and false-flag rate 0.347 at 0.5; recall at 5% and 10% false alarms 0.029 (threshold 0.988) and 0.212 (threshold 0.717); Class 7 recall 0.79 (50/63); validation AUROC 0.909; test_synth AUROC 0.701.

**Figures given for the run, verified** (`eval/analyze_final_v2.py`, output `results/final_v2/analysis.txt`; "natural" and "induced" are the origin of the row, correct rows included): natural 269 rows / 98 errors, AUROC 0.715 (95% CI 0.656-0.774), recall 0.55 and false-flag 0.30 at 0.5; induced 94 / 72, AUROC 0.650 (0.516-0.773), recall 0.88, false-flag 0.73; airline 0.730, retail 0.735, telecom 0.727 with telecom recall 0.31 (14/45) at 0.5 and false-flag 0.17. All as stated. Two things the figures do not show: the median score of correct rows is 0.07 overall but **0.63 for induced correct rows** (natural correct 0.03), and the 5% false-alarm threshold is 0.988, so the scores are no longer compressed as in final_v1: they are confident, and wrong with confidence in places (below).

**1. Paired comparison with the judges** (2,000 bootstrap resamples of the test rows of the subset, the same resample for both scores; judge scores from `results/judge_new_test/`; length-only fitted on the training split). Difference in AUROC, model minus the other:

| subset | model AUROC | vs gpt-5-mini | vs gpt-5-nano | vs length only |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **natural rows (headline)**, 98 errors, 171 correct | 0.715 (0.656-0.774) | **+0.021** (-0.075 to +0.121), better in 66.7% of resamples, p about 0.67 | +0.093 (-0.014 to +0.207), p about 0.09 | **+0.090 (+0.024 to +0.160), p about 0.011** |
| overall, 170 errors, 193 correct | 0.732 (0.678-0.782) | +0.013 (-0.064 to +0.085), p about 0.76 | **+0.113 (+0.023 to +0.196), p about 0.015** | +0.057 (-0.004 to +0.111), p about 0.07 |
| induced rows, 72 errors, 22 correct | 0.650 (0.516-0.773) | -0.048 (-0.213 to +0.116) | +0.064 (-0.142 to +0.244) | +0.091 (-0.053 to +0.228) |

The judges on the same rows: gpt-5-mini 0.695 (natural), 0.719 (overall), 0.698 (induced); gpt-5-nano 0.623, 0.619, 0.586; length only 0.626, 0.675, 0.559. **On the natural rows the model is not significantly better than gpt-5-mini** (+0.021, interval -0.075 to +0.121); it is significantly better than the length shortcut and, overall, than gpt-5-nano. Where it clearly beats gpt-5-mini is Class 7 (natural Class 7 against natural correct rows: model 0.780, mini 0.590, difference +0.190, interval +0.051 to +0.326; at 0.5 recall 0.78 against 0.38). The comparison with the length baseline overall is only just short of significance (p about 0.07), which is what the notebook's "beats length" statement would have to say.

**3. Operating points** (recall at the best threshold whose false-alarm rate on the correct rows of the subset is at most the target; the model has no advantage at low false alarms):

| subset | false alarms | model recall | gpt-5-mini recall |
|---|---|---|---|
| natural rows | 5% / 10% / 20% | 0.051 / 0.153 / 0.316 | 0.245 / 0.510 / 0.602 |
| all rows | 5% / 10% / 20% | 0.029 / 0.212 / 0.412 | 0.100 / 0.471 / 0.594 |
| Class 7 errors, all correct rows | 5% / 10% / 20% | 0.063 / 0.286 / 0.508 | 0.048 / 0.302 / 0.429 |
| natural Class 7, natural correct rows | 5% / 10% / 20% | 0.089 / 0.267 / 0.467 | 0.178 / 0.378 / 0.489 |

At the 0.5 threshold the model has recall 0.55 and false-flag 0.30 on natural rows (mini 0.53 and 0.135), and on Class 7 recall 0.79 against 0.37 (false-flag 0.35 against 0.145). **At a fixed, low false-alarm rate gpt-5-mini is much better than the model on natural rows and overall; only on Class 7 at 20% false alarms is the model ahead (0.508 against 0.429).**

**The 14 correct rows the model scores at or above 0.9** (of 193): all 14 are the same call, `cancel_reservation`, in airline, all from `test_real` (14 of its 77 correct rows), none with JSON-style agent text, with a median history of 13 turns (18 for all correct rows); gpt-5-mini scores them with a median 0.17. In the test set `cancel_reservation` appears 14 times as a correct call and 6 times as an error, and **the model scores all 20 with a median of 0.99, a tool-name shortcut**: in the training split the tool has 21 errors (16 real natural, 5 synthetic) against 6 correct rows (2 natural, 4 synthetic). These 14 rows are 7.3% of the correct rows and by themselves set the 5% false-alarm threshold at 0.988, which is why recall at 5% false alarms is 0.029.

**2. The induced shortcut: what induced rows share that natural rows lack.** Metadata first. The induced runs (`data/raw/induced/induced_runs.jsonl`) were made with **gpt-5-nano as the agent and gpt-4.1-mini as the user simulator**; the natural runs are the tau2-bench runs of claude-3-7-sonnet, gpt-4.1, gpt-4.1-mini and o4-mini with the benchmark's own user simulator (gpt-4.1). The run records carry `plan`, `user_model`, `agent_model`, `fault_fired`; **no field called `previous_*` exists** in the induced records, the candidates or the dataset rows (searched), and none of this metadata is part of the model input. **What the input does show** (`TOOLS`, `POLICY`, `EARLIER_CALLS`, then the history with `AGENT:` and `USER:` text, `AGENT_CALL`, `TOOL_RESULT`; messages are cut at 500 characters): (a) **the agent's text is a JSON object** (`{ "response": "..." }`, keys response, text or message) in 82 of the 94 induced test rows (18 of 22 correct, 64 of 72 errors) and in 2 of 269 natural rows (both correct); (b) **the agent's messages are long**: a median of 844 characters per message in induced rows against 261 in natural rows; (c) the user's messages are longer (median mean 266 against 157 characters) and the conversations have a few more calls (6 against 5 in the median); (d) injected events: a user correction in 49% of induced rows against 34% of natural rows, and a tool error in 36% against 17%. In training, JSON agent text occurs in 71 rows: 10 induced (6 errors, 4 correct), 22 tau2 gold (all correct), 38 synthetic (24 errors, 14 correct) and 1 natural error, so it is not a clean label signal there; the 13 induced training rows carry it nearly always and appear three times per epoch (`REAL_WEIGHT = 3`).

Tests on the final_v2 model, run locally on CPU with the downloaded checkpoint (`eval/ablate_induced_signature.py`, `results/final_v2/ablation_induced_signature.jsonl`; the inputs are rebuilt with the notebook's own formatting and score as the saved GPU predictions do):

| input edit | rows | score before to after (median; share >= 0.5) |
|---|---|---|
| induced rows, JSON agent text unwrapped to plain text | 18 correct / 64 errors | correct 0.67 to 0.59 (0.78 to 0.67); errors 0.68 to 0.64 (0.89 to 0.84) |
| induced rows, every agent message unwrapped and cut to 200 characters | 22 / 72 | correct 0.63 to 0.59 (0.73 to 0.68); errors 0.68 to 0.64 (0.88 to 0.88) |
| **natural rows, agent messages wrapped as `{ "response": ... }`** | 20 / 20 | **correct 0.00 to 0.03 (0.05 to 0.20), errors 0.51 to 0.70 (0.50 to 0.95); mean +0.16 and +0.17** |
| natural rows, every agent message cut to 200 characters (control) | 39 / 17 | correct 0.00 to 0.00 (0.18 to 0.18), errors 0.38 to 0.31 (0.47 to 0.41) |

**Reading.** The model does use the JSON style as an error cue: putting it on a natural row raises the score by about 0.17 and flags 95% of the natural errors and 20% of the correct rows. But **taking it (and the length) away from induced rows lowers their scores only by about 0.04 to 0.07**, so it explains only a small part of why induced correct rows score a median 0.63 (natural correct 0.03). Other pieces: the shift is older than the induced training rows (final_v1 had none and already scored induced correct rows at a median 0.44 against 0.30 for natural correct, 27% of them above the natural-correct 90th percentile; final_v2: 9%); within natural rows the score follows the length of the agent's messages (Spearman +0.55 for correct rows, +0.30 for errors); a user correction or a tool error in the input barely moves the scores (natural correct 0.11 with a correction, 0.01 without; induced correct 0.64 and 0.57). **Inside the induced rows the model has some real discrimination now (AUROC 0.650, 72 errors against 22 correct; final_v1: 0.503), and the 73% false-flag at 0.5 comes mostly from the whole group sitting high (median 0.63) with the threshold in the middle of it.** No single feature of the input explains it; it is a style-and-length shift of the kind that a model trained on 13 induced rows and mostly synthetic and natural-tau2 text cannot ignore.

**Fixes considered.** (i) *Remove the identifying features from the input* (implemented as the flag `NORMALIZE_AGENT_TEXT`, below): deterministic, applied to every split; it takes away the one cue shown to matter in the causal direction (JSON) and the length cue, but the ablation says that on its own it will move the induced scores only a little; its real value is robustness (a deployed agent that answers in JSON would otherwise raise the score of every call). (ii) *More induced correct handlings in training*: there are 5 in training against 8 errors, and the 177 train-split induced positives are about half wrong (22 of 48 confirmed overall, 6 of 12 for the propagation runs), so adding them unreviewed adds label noise; a human check of them (about 20 seconds each) is the clean version. (iii) *Exclude induced rows from training*: not a fix, because final_v1 had none and showed the shift, and it removes the only real Class 5 training errors (8). Recommendation: (i) first, then (ii) if the induced false-flag stays high.

**4. Weak spots: natural Class 4 and telecom.** *Natural Class 4* (16 errors: 13 telecom, 2 retail, 1 airline; found in `test_real` 7, queue 1 8, queue 2 1): recall 0.12 (2/16) at 0.5, median score 0.28 (gpt-5-mini: median 0.73). **All 16 are read-only lookups repeated with the same arguments** (15 of the 16 repeat an identical earlier call): `get_details_by_id` 7, `get_customer_by_phone` 5, `get_order_details` 2, `get_user_details` 1, `get_data_usage` 1. What the training data has: natural Class 4 22 rows (15 telecom, 4 retail, 3 airline; 20 of them read-only), against **synthetic Class 4 632 rows** (BFCL 384, retail 191, telecom 47, airline 10; only 120 read-only; 252 carry the injected `{"status": "success"}` result): the model learned "duplicate" from duplicated write calls with an artifact, and has 22 real examples (66 samples per epoch) of the harmless-looking repeated lookup that makes up the real Class 4. *Telecom* (81 rows, 45 errors: 41 natural, 4 induced): recall at 0.5 is 0.31, by class Class 1 0.12 (8), Class 3 0.33 (6), Class 4 0.14 (14), Class 7 0.50 (16), Class 5 1 of 1; 24 of the 45 errors are read-only calls; false-flag is 0.17. **Telecom's AUROC (0.727) equals the other domains', so the weak recall is mostly a threshold effect: telecom errors score lower (median 0.31) and 0.5 is too high there, not a failure of ranking.** Real data for telecom in training: 696 telecom rows (238 errors) of which 31 real errors (3 Class 1, 15 Class 4, 13 Class 7) and 182 real correct rows, against 207 synthetic errors; airline has 35 real errors and retail 28.

*Would spending $1.28 on the 531 remaining train-split runs (93 retail, 438 telecom) target this?* **Yes, directly.** From the yield of the 95 localized unflagged telecom runs, the 438 telecom runs should give about 92 Class 4 candidates (all kept by the rules; at the measured 73% first-labeler precision about 67 true Class 4 errors), about 67 Class 7 and 21 Class 1 errors after the rules, so **about 180 usable telecom errors against the 31 real ones now, and Class 4 in telecom rising from 15 to about 100**, plus roughly 660 real telecom correct rows (3.7 per candidate). It does not address the `cancel_reservation` shortcut (airline) or the induced shift. The estimate rests on 95 runs and on a first-labeler precision of 8 of 11 for Class 4, so it is loose; the cost stays $1.28 against the $1.95 balance and $0.09 above the tracked limit of `api_spend.json`. Not run; it needs your decision.

**5. Next experiments (at most two, one change each, about 1.7 hours each).**
1. **(a) `NORMALIZE_AGENT_TEXT` against the induced signature (implemented, default off).** Change: every assistant text turn is unwrapped from JSON and cut to 200 characters before the input is built, for training, validation, test and REAL_CV (`notebooks/normalize_agent_text_code.py`, flags `NORMALIZE_AGENT_TEXT = False` and `AGENT_TEXT_CHARS = 200` in the configuration cell, applied by `make_kaggle_notebooks.py`; a wrapper around `format_input_v2`, so nothing else in the notebook changes). Data and all other settings as in final_v2 (`REAL_WEIGHT = 3`, 3 epochs, one GPU, no new upload). Reason: section 2: the JSON style is the one cue shown to raise scores (+0.17 on natural rows), the agent's verbosity tracks the score within natural rows (+0.55), and both are visible in the input. Expected effect: small on the induced shift (the same edit on final_v2's inputs moves induced scores by 0.04 to 0.07), none on natural AUROC within about 0.02, and the end of the model's sensitivity to JSON-style agents; if the induced false-flag stays near 0.7 the cause is elsewhere and the next step is reviewed induced correct handlings. The 200-character cut can hide a confirmation request that sits late in a long agent message; the Class 7 recall and `no_confirmation` rows will show it. **Smoke test:** the whole notebook ran on a laptop CPU with a toy model (`TCV_SMOKE=1`) with the flag on and off, and the formatted input of an induced row has no JSON-style agent line left with the flag on (4 before) and 5 natural-row lines cut; nothing was run on a GPU. Settings in `docs/kaggle_runbook.md` section (g).
2. **(b) Hard negatives from successful runs, against the tool-name shortcut and for low false-alarm performance (not implemented; a data change).** Change: add correct calls of the tools that have more training errors than correct rows, taken from the successful (reward 1) simulations of the train-split tasks, with their real histories, through the same rules as the other correct rows. Reason: section 3: all 14 correct `cancel_reservation` test rows score at or above 0.9 and `cancel_reservation` has 21 errors against 6 correct rows in training, which alone fixes the 5% false-alarm threshold at 0.988 and recall at 5% false alarms at 0.029; the airline train-split tasks have 32 `cancel_reservation` calls in successful runs (and 82 in failed runs), so the 6 can become about 38, and `modify_pending_order_payment` (6 errors, 0 correct) can be treated the same way. Expected effect: recall at 5% and 10% false alarms on natural rows rising from 0.05 and 0.15 (gpt-5-mini 0.25 and 0.51), with natural AUROC steady; the risk is that reward 1 does not certify every call, so the rules and a spot check are needed. It needs the builder change and a rebuilt training file (the test files stay identical), then one run with the same settings as (a)'s baseline.

## D-028 — Two dataset versions: v3a tool balancing and v3b more real errors (2026-11-21)

Both are built by `data/build_final_dataset.py --variant v3a|v3b --review-complete --write` into `data/derived/final_v3a/` and `final_v3b/` (gitignored, like the others; the variant `v2` of D-026 is still the default). `NORMALIZE_AGENT_TEXT` stays off (shelved; its flag and code remain). **Rows are added after the validation split is fixed, to the training split only, from task groups that are in none of validation, the held-out synthetic test and the test set**, so `val.jsonl`, `test.jsonl` and `test_synth.jsonl` are byte-identical to the v2 build in both versions (SHA-256 below) and no task group straddles two splits.

**v3a: tool balancing (no API).** Source: the pre-run tau2-bench results (`data/raw/tau2-bench/.../results/final`), simulations with reward 1 on train-split tasks outside validation, the held-out test and the test set: 2,899 successful of 4,384 simulations. For every tool whose training errors outnumber its correct rows, successful-run calls of that tool are added until correct rows equal errors or the runs have no more eligible calls, worst-imbalanced tools first (by errors per correct row), within 25% of the training size (cap 1,565 rows); calls are taken round-robin over runs so that no run dominates. A call is used only if it passes the rules of D-026 (`screen_positive`: not an identical repeat of an earlier call, no structural Class 7 signal, not a wrong line lookup), its result was not a tool error, the tool is not a free-text tool, and its content is not already in any split (the same input format as every other row: `history()`, 2,000-character tool results, the tool schema text). Rows: `origin: natural`, `source: tau2_success`, `real_origin: true`, `label_tier: tau2_success_gated`, ids `tau2_success::<run key>::C<k>`.

Result: **143 rows added = 2.3% of the training rows, far below the 25% cap, because the deficit is small.** 49 tools had more errors than correct rows (a deficit of 292 rows before the final de-duplication); 39 of them, with 146 rows of deficit, are BFCL, CriticTool or other non-tau2 tools with no successful tau2 run, and stay as they are. Tools balanced (errors / correct before to after): `exchange_delivered_order_items` 406 / 321 to 406 (85 added; 114 candidates were already in the dataset as gold rows), `cancel_reservation` 21 / 6 to 23 (17 added), `modify_pending_order_items` 220 / 200 to 220 (20), `return_delivered_order_items` 134 / 126 to 134 (8), `modify_pending_order_payment` 6 / 0 to 6 (6), `resume_line` 28 / 24 to 28 (4), `suspend_line` 3 / 0 to 1 (only one successful call exists), `disable_roaming` 3 / 2 to 3, `get_data_usage` 9 / 8 to 9. The 143 rows are retail 119, airline 17, telecom 7. After: 41 tools still have more errors than correct rows (149 rows of deficit), all but a few BFCL or other-API tools (`FilmTMDB.get_movie_details` 22 / 4, `GoogleScholar.get_author_id` 20 / 7, ...); `book_reservation` 23 / 22 stays (9 candidates were duplicates). `eval/tool_balance_report.py` prints the full table (`results/final_v2/tool_balance_v3a.txt`).

**Tool-only baseline (AUROC of the label predicted from the tool name alone, statistics from the training split of each version):**

| version | train rows | train, in-sample | validation | test | test, natural rows |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| v2 (final_v2 data) | 6,231 | 0.690 | 0.672 | 0.678 | 0.671 |
| v3a | 6,374 | 0.673 | 0.669 | 0.707 | 0.689 |

**Two readings that were not the aim.** (1) The shortcut inside training is only a little weaker (0.690 to 0.673) and on validation it is unchanged (0.672 to 0.669): balancing the few tau2 tools cannot remove a tool-name signal that is spread over 49 tools, most of them in BFCL. (2) **On the test set the tool-only baseline went up (0.678 to 0.707)**: the test set has more errors than correct rows for the balanced tools (`exchange_delivered_order_items` 9 correct / 17 errors, `modify_pending_order_items` 11 / 17, `modify_pending_order_payment` 0 / 4), so a training prior that is closer to 50% / 50% fits it better, while for `cancel_reservation` (test 14 correct / 6 errors) it removes a prior that was wrong. **The tool name alone separates the test rows with AUROC 0.68 to 0.71, as high as the model's natural-row AUROC (0.715) minus a few hundredths**, so a model that only learned tool frequencies would already be close to it; this is a baseline the model has to be compared with in every report from now on.
