**Also changed:** README "Project structure" lists `docs/writeup.md`, `docs/model_card.md`, `docs/licenses.md`, `docs/demo_plan.md` and `verifier/`; the DECISIONS line there now says D-001 to D-031.

**Summary of D-031 and what needs a decision from you.** (1) `docs/writeup.md`, `docs/model_card.md`, `docs/licenses.md`, `verifier/predict.py` (+ README usage) and `docs/demo_plan.md` exist and are committed; none is published. (2) The inference package reproduces the training input exactly (363 of 363) and the saved scores to 0.0013. (3) **Open before anything is published:** the license of our own code, labels and weights (TODO in the model card); the terms of OpenAI and Anthropic for the model-generated text in τ²-bench transcripts, induced runs and synthetic data (not read); CriticTool's provenance; whether the personal-looking values in τ²-bench rows are synthetic; and publishing from a fresh git history (the 37 MB `induced_runs.jsonl` is in the current one). (4) The thresholds shipped with the model are set on the test set. (5) Hand-made probes show the score is phrasing-sensitive and misses a clear policy violation: the card, the README and the writeup say so; a systematic robustness test is not done.

---

## D-032 — Publishing: licenses, public test set, clean release repo, Hugging Face prep, demo v1 (2026-11-22)

No API call and no upload. **Your decisions:** Apache-2.0 for the code and the ModernBERT v3b weights; CC BY 4.0 for our human labels; the test set published with τ²-bench's MIT notice and attribution; the training data not published (which also keeps CriticTool rows out of anything redistributed); release from a fresh git history. They are recorded in `docs/licenses.md` section 6.

**Step 1: license files.** Added: `LICENSE` (the canonical Apache License 2.0 text, taken from the BFCL repository's `LICENSE`, which is the unmodified text with the appendix template); `NOTICE` (project, what Apache-2.0 covers, that the weights are a modified ModernBERT-base, the four third-party projects; **the copyright holder line is `<COPYRIGHT HOLDER: fill in before publishing>`: I did not put a name in**); `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` (for τ²-bench MIT "Copyright (c) 2025 Sierra Research", BFCL/Gorilla Apache-2.0, ModernBERT-base Apache-2.0 with the authors' citation request (arXiv:2412.13663; the card gives no copyright line, said so), Qwen2.5-1.5B Apache-2.0 "Copyright 2024 Alibaba Cloud" (comparison model only, no Qwen weights published), and a paragraph that the published weights were trained on unpublished data that includes CriticTool rows); `licenses/` with the texts (`Apache-2.0.txt`, `BFCL-Gorilla-Apache-2.0.txt`, `tau2-bench-MIT.txt` copied from `data/raw/`, `Qwen2.5-1.5B-Apache-2.0.txt` fetched from its repository, `CC-BY-4.0.txt` fetched from creativecommons.org); `DATA_LICENSE.md` for the test set (three layers: our labels CC BY 4.0, τ²-bench content MIT with the notice reproduced verbatim (checked by text comparison against the upstream file), the BFCL-based rows of `test_synth` Apache-2.0 and modified by us, plus our synthetic modifications CC BY 4.0), with an attribution paragraph and three things it cannot settle (model-generated text and the providers' terms, personal data, no warranty). **Model card:** front matter `license: apache-2.0`, the license row filled, the paragraph on training data rewritten (not published; CriticTool recorded; model-generated-text terms stated as not reviewed); developer and citation stay TODO. `docs/licenses.md` gets section 6 with the decisions. **What your decisions do not resolve, and I did not pretend they do:** the OpenAI and Anthropic terms for model-generated text (licenses.md 3.2) now also apply to the published test set and to the weights; `DATA_LICENSE.md` and the model card say it is not reviewed and that users must check it for their own use.

**Step 2: the public test set, `release/test_set/`** (built by `scripts/build_test_set_release.py` from `data/derived/final/test.jsonl` and `test_synth.jsonl`, whose SHA-256 are those of D-026: `f1c20d8e...` and `fb2b869b...`). `test.jsonl` (363 rows, 4.27 MB) and `test_synth.jsonl` (374 rows, 3.71 MB), each row with only: `id`, `domain`, `conversation`, `candidate_call`, `tool_schemas_text` (needed to rebuild the model input; I added it to the fields you listed), `label`, `error_class`, `error_class_name`, `origin`, `class7_reason`, `class7_reason_source`, `synthetic`. Dropped: review notes, annotator notes, run keys, agent model names, provenance fields, gold actions, corruption details. **The sub-reasons are heuristic**: 62 of the 63 Class 7 test rows have a heuristic tag and 1 a human one (D-024); the README says so. `release/test_set/README.md` has the label scheme, the field table, the composition (by class and origin: Class 1 30 = 21 + 9, 2: 4 = 0 + 4, 3: 18 = 14 + 4, 4: 19 = 16 + 3, 5: 20 = 1 + 19, 6: 16 = 1 + 15, 7: 63 = 45 + 18; 171 natural and 22 induced correct; airline 116, retail 166, telecom 81), a summary of the labeling guide and its two rules, how the set was built and reviewed (sources, proposals by a model, the human queues, the limits: one reviewer, not adjudicated, induced signature), the license pointer and a citation TODO.

**Personal-data check** (`release/test_set/pii_check.txt`, values masked): searched for e-mail, phone, Luhn-valid card-like numbers, SSN-like numbers, IBAN, street addresses, dates of birth (only near "birth", "DOB", "born") and capitalized pairs that look like names, comparing each hit with τ²-bench's own databases, tasks and policies (`data/raw/tau2-bench/data/tau2/domains/`). **Found, real rows plus the τ²-bench rows of `test_synth`:** no phone, card, SSN or IBAN pattern outside the records. E-mail: 584 occurrences (55 distinct), all in the records except `youremail@example.com`, a placeholder in a tool result. Street addresses: 1,036 occurrences (75 distinct), all in the records except "123 Maple Street, Springfield", a generic address an LLM agent wrote in an induced run. Dates of birth: all in the records except four typed during runs: `1970-01-01` for "John Smith" (an agent's placeholder in a telecom lookup), `1959-04-15` (the simulated user, induced telecom run) and two airline dates (2012, 2015) that the simulated user gave for two children in one o4-mini run (traced: the user turn, and neither date is in the airline database; the first names are). Name-like pairs not explained by the benchmark's vocabulary: phrases ("Preferred Network", "Galaxy Tab"), "Robert Frost" (a user quoting a poem), "John Doe", "John Smith". **BFCL rows (198, no reference records):** no e-mail, phone, SSN, IBAN or street address; one Luhn-valid 16-digit string, `2345-6789-1234-5678` (sequential digits from BFCL's own scenario), a few dates, and fictional names ("Maxwell Edison", "Lara Croft"). **The check is pattern-based and cannot prove there is no real personal data**; the README says that and asks for an issue if any is found. **I changed the check while building it:** the first version flagged every ISO date as a date of birth (4,977 hits) and every capitalized pair as a possible name (315 unmatched); I restricted dates to the neighbourhood of the words "birth", "dob", "born" and counted a pair as explained when both words occur in the benchmark's text, and looked at what remained by hand. The explained-by-vocabulary rule can hide a real name whose two words happen to occur in the benchmark; that is a limit.

**Step 3: clean release repository, `scripts/make_release.sh`, and the secret scans.** The script exports `git archive HEAD` (only committed, tracked files; it warns when tracked files are modified) to `../toolcall-verifier-release/` (`--force` replaces it), **removes** `data/raw/`, `data/derived/` (training pools, negatives, controls, CriticTool conversion, review queues and decisions, localization caches, induced plans and candidates, final splits), `data/kaggle_upload*/`, `data/labels_manual.jsonl` (first hand labels with reviewer notes), `models/`, `checkpoints/`, `.env`, the raw judge outputs (`results/judge_new_test/openai-{mini,nano}.jsonl`, `results/phase0_raw/`, `results/speed_cache/`), the executed `docs/kaggle_v2_run.ipynb` and any file over 5 MB (none needed removal by size), then creates a **new repository with one commit** ("Initial release", author `toolcall-verifier <noreply@example.invalid>` unless `RELEASE_AUTHOR_NAME` and `RELEASE_AUTHOR_EMAIL` are set), and runs gitleaks if available. Nothing is pushed. **Result of the run: 209 files exported, 52 removed (46 of `data/derived`, `data/labels_manual.jsonl`, the 4 raw judge files, the executed notebook), 157 kept, 15 MB, `.git` 3.1 MB; the largest files are the two test-set files (4.3 and 3.7 MB).** The raw induced runs (`data/raw/induced/induced_runs.jsonl`, 37 MB) are in no file of the release and in no commit of it.

*Scanner:* gitleaks 8.30.1 (darwin arm64; downloaded from the project's GitHub release into the session's scratch folder, SHA-256 `b40ab0ae...a5` checked against the published `checksums.txt`; not installed system-wide). Run with `--redact=100`; no value was printed. **(a) Full history of the current repository (117+ commits, 10 of them with findings): 552 findings, all rule `generic-api-key`, in 12 data files** (the training pools `data/derived/{negatives_pool_final,bfcl_pool,negatives_pool,negatives_class2_class5,positives_class2_controls,positives_class4_controls,negatives_llm,positives_class6_controls,external_critictool}.jsonl`, `results/judge_new_test/openai-{nano,mini}.jsonl` and the new `release/test_set/test_synth.jsonl`), **none in code, configuration or documents**. By key name: `access_token` 496 (7 distinct values), `access code` 30 (1 value, 5 digits), `prompt_key` 21 (15 distinct 16-hex-digit cache keys of our own judge runs), `password` 5 (8 distinct values in the data). **Triage by shape, without printing values:** none begins with a known secret prefix (`sk-`, `ghp_`, `AKIA`, `xox`, `AIza`, `hf_`, `Bearer`); the `access_token` values are 12 to 13-character strings, 3 of 7 found literally in the BFCL or τ²-bench source data and the other 4 start with `abc` or `cor` (placeholders from BFCL scenarios and from our own synthetic Class 2 corruptions); the passwords are of the same kind (6 of 8 found in the raw benchmark data, the others start `mic` and `cor`); the `prompt_key` values are hashes that our judge cache wrote. **My conclusion: false positives on benchmark placeholders; no real credential found. This is a judgment from shapes and prefixes, not a proof**: the scan covers every file type but a real key embedded in a prompt text would look the same as these. **(b) The release copy (files, then its single-commit history): 19 findings, all the same rule in `release/test_set/test_synth.jsonl`** (BFCL scenario placeholders: 16 `access_token` ("abc..." values, 12 and 13 characters), 2 `access code`, 1 `password`); a `.gitleaks.toml` allowlist for that one file with the reason is part of the release (default rules otherwise), and with it both scans of the release copy report 0. The earlier search for `sk-` patterns in non-`.jsonl` history files (D-031) found nothing either.

*Personal identifiers found in the release copy and handled:* three text files contained your local user name (`results/final_v1/train_composition_before_after.txt`, a home path) or your Kaggle user name (`docs/qwen_v3b_run_log.txt`, `docs/final_qwen_v3b_eval_report.txt`, the notebook path). No e-mail address and no other path. **The script replaces such strings in the copy only**, from a local, ignored file `.release-scrub` (`pattern=>replacement` per line; it is in `.gitignore` and in the deny list, because a tracked list would publish what it hides); I created it with the three patterns, and after the scrub the copy has none (checked by grep). The working repository still has them. **The git history of the working repository is not touched.** `README.md` now says what the published repository contains and what it does not.

**Step 4: Hugging Face prep, `release/hf_model/` (nothing uploaded).** Built by `scripts/prepare_hf_model.py` (asserts that `models/final_v3b/model.safetensors` has the SHA-256 recorded in D-029, `617accea...04c24`): `model.safetensors` (598,439,784 bytes; **not tracked by git**, `.gitignore` and the release deny list), `config.json`, `tokenizer.json`, `tokenizer_config.json`, `train_info.json`, `README.md` (= `docs/model_card.md`, Apache-2.0 in the front matter; relative links turned into links to `--repo-url`, default `https://github.com/OWNER/REPO`; the usage block changed to `snapshot_download` + `from predict import load, score`; a "Files in this repository" section added; no relative link is left), `predict.py` (a copy of `verifier/predict.py`) with `data/` (policy text, tool-schema text, thresholds), `LICENSE`, `NOTICE`. **Check:** with the working directory `release/hf_model` and its own `predict.py`, 8 randomly chosen rows of the **public** `release/test_set/test.jsonl` (so the public format works with the package) give probabilities within 0.0013 of the saved ones. `release/test_set/` was also made self-contained for a separate dataset upload: it now carries `DATA_LICENSE.md` and the three license texts it refers to, and its README links to them locally.

**The commands are in `release/UPLOAD_COMMANDS.md`. One difference from what you asked:** the installed `huggingface_hub` (2.4.0) has **no `huggingface-cli`; the CLI is `hf`** (checked with `hf --help`, `hf auth`, `hf repos create --help`, `hf upload --help`). The file uses `hf auth login`, `hf repos create HF_USERNAME/toolcall-verifier-modernbert-v3b --type model --public` and `hf upload HF_USERNAME/toolcall-verifier-modernbert-v3b release/hf_model . --repo-type model --commit-message ...`, the same for a dataset repository for the test set and for the Space, and names the `huggingface-cli` equivalents for an old install. **Placeholders you must fill before upload:** `HF_USERNAME`, the code repository URL (`--repo-url`), the copyright holder in `NOTICE` (`<COPYRIGHT HOLDER ...>`), the developer and citation TODOs of the card and the test-set README. I did not run any `hf` command that talks to the network.

**Step 5: demo v1, `release/space/` (Gradio, CPU; not uploaded).** `scripts/build_space.py` picks **12 real test rows from the public test set by fixed rules** and writes `examples.json`; `app.py`, `README.md` (Space front matter: gradio 6.32.0, `app_file: app.py`, Apache-2.0) and `requirements.txt` (CPU torch from the PyTorch index, transformers, huggingface_hub) are hand-written; `predict.py`, `data/`, `LICENSE`, `NOTICE`, `DATA_LICENSE.md` and the two license texts it cites are copied. **The 12 rows** (smallest conversation of at least 1,500 characters that matches each slot; order shuffled with a fixed seed so the list does not give the answer away; titles are neutral, the label appears after scoring): **6 errors the model catches at the 10% false-alarm threshold (0.7314)**: Class 4 natural telecom `get_customer_by_phone` (p 0.999), Class 7 natural telecom `refuel_data` (0.794), Class 3 natural telecom `send_payment_request` (1.000), Class 1 natural airline `book_reservation` (0.934), Class 5 induced retail `get_order_details` (0.926), Class 6 induced airline `update_reservation_flights` (0.998); **3 it misses**: Class 7 natural telecom `refuel_data` (0.025, the same tool as the catch above, on purpose), Class 1 natural retail `modify_pending_order_items` (0.053), Class 3 natural airline `get_flight_status` (0.001); **3 correct calls**: two allowed (retail `get_order_details`, airline `get_reservation_details`, both 0.000) and **one false alarm** (airline `cancel_reservation`, 0.999: the shortcut of D-027 and D-029). Of the 56 errors the model catches in the whole test set, 28 have conversations under 9,000 characters; the mix of 6/3/3 is a design choice and **not** the model's hit rate (it catches 56 of 170 errors at this threshold), which the app says. Class 2 has no example (4 test rows, all induced). Telecom is over-represented among the catches because the natural catches of Classes 3 and 7 are all telecom.

*The app:* a neutral-title dropdown of the 12 examples, a domain dropdown, a threshold selector (fa10 default, fa05, 0.5), an editable conversation (one line per turn: `USER:`, `AGENT:`, `CALL: {json}`, `RESULT:`; other lines continue the previous turn) and an editable candidate call (JSON), a Score button and a reset button. **Result:** FLAG or ALLOW, the score and threshold, a P(incorrect) / P(correct) bar, **the policy text the model saw for the tool** (or a note that the tool has none: policy text exists for 18 write tools), the **human label** (label, class, origin, Class 7 sub-reason marked as a heuristic tag, and whether this row is caught, missed, allowed or a false alarm at the 10% threshold) together with the saved GPU probability and the live CPU one, and the exact model input in a collapsed box. **A visible "Known limitations" panel (open by default):** curated examples, about one error in three caught at this setting, the in-sample threshold, the small test set and the not-significant comparison with GPT-5-mini, the brittleness probes (0.02 / 0.88 / 0.9999 and the 0.015 miss), the `cancel_reservation` shortcut, edited inputs, scope. **Editing is allowed**: an unedited example is scored from its stored row (so the score equals the evaluation run's), an edited one is parsed from the text with the bundled tool-schema text of the domain, and the page then says that no human label applies and that edited inputs may behave unpredictably.

*Tests (local, CPU, gradio 6.32.0 + `gradio_client`, the real model):* all 12 examples through the app's `/score` endpoint: **live probability within 0.0038 of the saved one (8 of 12 within 0.0001), decisions and outcomes as expected** (6 FLAG, 6 ALLOW; the 3 misses allowed, the false alarm flagged); policy text shown for the 7 examples whose tool has a snippet and the "no policy text" note for the other 5; an edited conversation gives a score and the "no human label" text; the 0.5 threshold works; a malformed candidate call gives a readable error; **per-click latency on this Mac: median 0.4 s, maximum 2.0 s** (a 10-core CPU; the free Space CPU will be slower and I did not measure it); the page rendered in the built-in browser and the Score button worked. **Not tested:** the build on Hugging Face (the requirements file, the `sdk_version`, the model download from the Hub), concurrent users, a phone layout, and the Space's first-start time. The line-to-turns parser is exact for unedited examples (stored rows are used) and is not guaranteed to round-trip every conversation that contains a line starting with `USER:` inside a message (only affects edited text). The app code assumes the Hub model repository named in `MODEL_REPO` (default `HF_USERNAME/toolcall-verifier-modernbert-v3b`).

*Release folder rerun:* `scripts/make_release.sh --force` was re-run on the final commit: **240 files exported, 52 removed, 188 kept, 20 MB (`.git` 3.9 MB), one commit by the generic author; no weights, no `data/derived`, no `data/raw`, no file over 5 MB, none of your identifiers; gitleaks over the files and over the new history: 0 findings** (with the allowlist for the BFCL placeholders in `test_synth.jsonl`). The copy is at `../toolcall-verifier-release/`; nothing was pushed. The upload commands for the model, the dataset and the Space are in `release/UPLOAD_COMMANDS.md`.

**Part C (reframing of the results; no number changed).** Your instruction: lead with the model's strengths and keep the statistical caveat in one place. **Wording used** in `README.md`, `docs/writeup.md`, `docs/model_card.md` (and so `release/hf_model/README.md`, regenerated) and the Space (`app.py`, `README.md`): "A ~150M-parameter model that runs on a laptop CPU in about 0.7 s, at no API cost per call, exceeds GPT-5-mini at catching real agent mistakes (AUROC 0.771 vs 0.719) and significantly beats GPT-5-nano (0.619) and the length shortcut (0.675), and beats the tool-name shortcut (0.716) overall", followed by "It scores a call in about 0.7 s on a laptop CPU against 3.9 s for GPT-5-mini, with no API cost and conversations that never leave your machine." **Two adaptations of your sentence, for accuracy:** (1) "~150M" (the model has 149M parameters); (2) the tool-name shortcut is "beaten overall" and not "significantly beaten": the paired interval is +0.055 (+0.001 to +0.108) overall and includes zero on natural rows (-0.016 to +0.111), so "significantly" would overstate it; a results-section sentence says this in numbers. **The statistical note** ("On this 363-row test set, the difference from GPT-5-mini is within the confidence interval (paired 95% CI includes zero); a larger test set would be needed to confirm the gap.") is now in the results section once and in the limitations once of the README, the writeup and the model card, and once in the Space's limitations panel (the Space has no results section); the earlier repetitions ("not significantly better than GPT-5-mini" in the intro, summaries, the model card's first paragraph, `DECISIONS.md`, the demo plan) were removed or reworded, and the phrase no longer appears unqualified outside this log (the entries above are the historical record and were not edited). **Kept on purpose, because they are numbers and not the caveat you named:** the table columns for recall at 5% and 10% false alarms (GPT-5-mini 0.471 against 0.329 at 10%), one sentence of that comparison in the README limitations, the model card, the writeup and the Space panel, the "triage signal, not a gate" sentence, the shortcut paragraph and the Qwen subset findings. ## D-033 — Publication: GitHub, Hugging Face model and dataset live; hosted Space pending (2026-11-22)

**Live URLs.** GitHub https://github.com/Shalini-Srivastavaaa/toolcall-verifier (public, Apache-2.0 detected, topics llm-agents, tool-calling, ai-safety, modernbert, evaluation, verifier); model https://huggingface.co/ShaliniSrivastava/toolcall-verifier-modernbert-v3b (public, apache-2.0); dataset https://huggingface.co/datasets/ShaliniSrivastava/toolcall-verifier-test-set (public, `license: other`). **Not published: the Space** (`ShaliniSrivastava/toolcall-verifier-demo`).

**Release copy.** Rebuilt with `scripts/make_release.sh` and author `Shalini Srivastava <159019629+Shalini-Srivastavaaa@users.noreply.github.com>`; gitleaks 8.30.1 over files and history: 0 findings (each rebuild). The GitHub repository has two commits (the initial release and a cross-link commit, pushed normally, no force); both carry the noreply author, shown against the Shalini-Srivastavaaa account.

**Verified.** *Model:* 12 files on the Hub, `model.safetensors` SHA-256 equal to the expected value, card license apache-2.0, and a fresh `snapshot_download` into a temp folder scored 3 public test rows (telecom, airline, retail) with `predict.py` within 0.00002 of the saved scores (limit 0.002). *Dataset:* 9 files; the Hub rejected the first upload because `license_link` was a relative path (`license: other` needs an https URL), so it is now the absolute URL of `DATA_LICENSE.md` on the Hub; 363 real rows, 170 incorrect (counted locally in the uploaded `test.jsonl`). The two files have different columns, so the card declares two configs, `real` (default) and `synthetic`. *GitHub:* README renders, the results chart (`docs/figures/auroc_comparison.png`) loads (1530 px wide), the Mermaid diagram renders, the license is detected as Apache-2.0. *Local demo:* `release/space/app.py` started from the pinned requirements, downloaded the model from the Hub and scored example 1 at 0.0253 (saved 0.0252, ALLOW, a known miss) and example 5 at 0.9976 (saved 0.9976, FLAG) through the Gradio client; this ran in an existing venv with the same pinned versions, not a fresh one.

**Space pending.** Creating a Gradio Space on the free cpu-basic tier returned 402 Payment Required (needs Hugging Face PRO); nothing was created and no workaround was tried. `release/space/` stays in the repository with pinned requirements (torch 2.15.0, transformers 5.24.0, huggingface_hub 2.4.0, safetensors 0.8.0; gradio 6.32.0 from `sdk_version`, `python_version` 3.12), so the demo can be run locally (README section "Run the demo locally") or uploaded later with the commands in `docs/release_steps.md` step 4, now marked NOT DONE. The README badge and links to a hosted Space were removed. Those two docs mention the Space commands and the "NOT DONE" note and are in the public repository (`docs/release_steps.md`, `release/UPLOAD_COMMANDS.md`).

**Dataset viewer.** Not confirmed. The datasets-server answered "busier than usual" on 9 attempts over several minutes; after the card declared the two configs it listed the splits (`real` and `synthetic`, each `test`) but `is-valid` still reported viewer false with the size jobs pending. To check later: https://huggingface.co/datasets/ShaliniSrivastava/toolcall-verifier-test-set.

**Cross-links.** README: model and dataset badges and a dataset link next to the model link; model card: link to GitHub and the dataset; dataset card: links to GitHub and the model; only the changed cards were re-uploaded.

**Open.** The README install line lists torch (`pip install torch transformers huggingface_hub`); the project `.venv` itself has no torch (an earlier venv was used for the checks). The terms for model-generated text (`docs/licenses.md` 3.2) are still unreviewed.

## D-034 — Provenance section in the dataset card; provider terms recorded as reviewed (2026-11-22)

**Dataset card.** `release/test_set/README.md` has a "Provenance" section: the conversations and tool calls are model-generated (269 `natural` rows: τ²-bench simulations by gpt-4.1, gpt-4.1-mini, o4-mini, claude-3-7-sonnet with a gpt-4.1 user simulator; 94 `induced` rows: our runs with gpt-5-nano as agent and gpt-4.1-mini as user simulator; `test_synth`: scripted corruptions of benchmark conversations), the error labels of the 363 real rows are human decisions, the `test_synth` labels are true by construction and not human-reviewed, and the text is shared under τ²-bench's MIT license together with the providers' output terms. The intro sentence no longer calls the calls "real tool calls from agents" without saying the conversations are model-generated; the README and the model card say the same. Origin counts (269 and 94) were counted in `test.jsonl`; the model names are those of D-010, D-018, D-019 and D-027.

**Licenses.** `docs/licenses.md` 3.2 changed from "not read / unreviewed" to: reviewed by the project owner on 2026-11-22, outputs owned by the user under the OpenAI and Anthropic terms, no competing-model use. This is the owner's conclusion; I did not read the terms and the section says so, and it is not legal advice. The older text is kept for the record. The statements "terms not reviewed" in the README, `DATA_LICENSE.md` (three copies), the model card and `DECISIONS.md` were changed to match. Not looked for: the τ²-bench authors' own statement about their transcripts.

**Upload.** Only the dataset card (`README.md`) was re-uploaded to the Hub, as asked; the Hub copies of `DATA_LICENSE.md` and the model card still say "not reviewed" until they are re-uploaded. **Update:** both were re-uploaded afterwards at the owner's request, so the Hub, GitHub and the source repository now say the same. The GitHub repository was updated by a new commit. **Dataset viewer (open in D-033): working** on 2026-11-22 after this upload (`is-valid`: viewer, preview, search, filter and statistics all true; configs `real` and `synthetic`).

## D-035 — Wording about the test labels and other overstated sentences corrected (2026-11-27)

A check against D-022 showed sentences that said more than the records support. **Test labels:** "every label is a human decision" was too strong. Every test row was reviewed by a human, but written rules adjusted some labels without a second look: queue 5 was applied by rule (34 calls marked wrong became correct, all 34 in the final test set; one error candidate removed), and the line-probe rule removed 26 accepted errors. The dataset card also said the Class 7 decisions were re-made and the correct calls each confirmed by a human; both corrected. The README, `DECISIONS.md`, the writeup, the model card, the dataset card and the three `DATA_LICENSE.md` copies now say this. **Writeup:** the licensing bullet still said the provider terms were unread and nothing was published; updated. **README:** "Real labeled data was the lever" became "appears to have been", and the headline edit the owner made on GitHub was copied into this repository. No data, label or number changed.
